- **The Mac Mini's copy of `voice-samples.md` is truncated to exactly 65,535 bytes** (found 2026-09-03 while reviewing #229). The MacBook Pro's copy is 93,848 bytes with a different MD5. **65,535 is exactly 2^16 - 1**, which is a truncation signature rather than an older honest revision — something on that host clipped the file at a 64KiB boundary. The file is git-ignored under rule 9, so git cannot have done it; the suspect is whatever copy/sync path put it there. Find what truncated it before removing anything. The MacBook Pro is the authoritative host for the private layer as of 2026-09-03. - **Amend `content-oracle` SKILL.md with the three probe failure modes found in probation week 1** (2026-08-30). The skill documents one form of the gravity problem (a culturally louder neighbour dragging a probe off its mechanism). Week 1 found two more, each needing a different countermeasure; both are recorded with evidence in `creative-studio/content-machine/ideas-bank.md` under the 2026-08-30 run. **(2) Bigger claimant** — a word a larger community owns in a different sense; `interviewing` routed the probe to `['interviews','jobsearchhack','devopsjobs']`. The countermeasure is free: the engine prints its discovered subreddits to stderr *before* it searches, so a mis-aimed run can be killed at that line at no cost. **(3) Stopword collapse** — the query's most distinctive-looking word is actually a high-frequency function word; `telling` returned ten unrelated X posts and `only` returned a fishing video at 437k views. Countermeasure is to ask what a keyword matcher would do with the query's commonest content word. Also worth folding in: YouTube has a second failure mode the skill does not cover — it collapsed to *unrelated entertainment* (a BL mafia-romance channel; a true-crime video with mattress ads) on an honest date window with zero relevance, which is distinct from the documented collapse-to-category-roundup. Deliberately not edited during the run: the skill's own rule is to let the bank accumulate evidence and amend by use rather than by argument, so this is a separate deliberate pass. — assigned: Sean

- **The claim-6 commit guard and silent-disarm warning are `.git/hooks/` on the Mac Mini ONLY — a fresh clone, or any `.git` rebuild, has neither** (installed 2026-08-31, before B3's window). `.git/hooks/pre-commit` blocks committing `agents-sdk/config.toml` while the drill is armed (the registration is a personal device name in a public repo, and committing it turns `main` red via `tests/test_claim6_schedule.py`); `.git/hooks/post-merge` warns when a merge has disarmed the drill while `com.sean.agent.claim6-drill` is still loaded — the silent-disarm case. Both are untracked by design and both fail open. Live values backed up outside the repo at `~/Code-Brain/backups/claim6-registration-2026-08-31.txt`. **The durable fix remains unbuilt**: a gitignored local-override file the config loader reads (e.g. `agents-sdk/config.local.toml` merged over `config.toml`), so the tracked config can stay unarmed and committable and the registration stops depending on one machine's dirty working tree plus two hooks nobody can see in `git status`. Second known gap: both hooks grep the WHOLE file for `schedule_enabled = true` rather than scoping to `[agents.claim6_drill]` — harmless today (measured: that string occurs exactly once in the file, and `[agents.vault_critic]` is the only other `schedule_enabled` key, set false), but arming any other agent would make `post-merge` miss a real claim-6 disarm. — assigned: Sean
- **Two contract tests are now permanently red on any host where the claim-6 drill is armed** (found 2026-08-31 while arming B3 on the Mini). `tests/test_claim6_schedule.py::test_claim6_schedule_is_unregistered_and_default_disabled` and `tests/test_claim6_drill.py::test_meta_agent_report_contains_drill_block_before_delivery` both read the REAL `agents-sdk/config.toml` and assert it is unarmed (`schedule_enabled is False`, blank label/device, and a report block reading `DRILL NOT REGISTERED`). They fail because the deploy SUCCEEDED. Suite went 1047 passed → **1045 passed, 2 failed**, and both failures are this one cause. Two consequences worth ruling on: (1) these tests make `config.toml` **uncommittable while armed** — committing the registration would turn `main` red, which is the mechanism enforcing the runbook's "production registration belongs in the private deploy revision"; (2) the Mini's suite is now permanently 2-red, so a *real* regression during B3's window is easier to wave off as "just the known two." The contract test cannot distinguish "unarmed because it ships unarmed" from "unarmed because someone reverted the deploy." Suggested fix (MacBook Pro, not the Mini — no code edits on the deploy host): make the assertion conditional on the committed HEAD blob rather than the working-tree file, e.g. assert against `git show HEAD:agents-sdk/config.toml`, so it keeps proving the shipped artifact is disarmed while staying green on a registered host. — assigned: Sean
- **The armed claim-6 registration lives ONLY in the Mini's uncommitted working tree, and a future `git merge --ff-only` touching `config.toml` will refuse or silently strand it** (2026-08-31). `agents-sdk/config.toml` is now a dirty tracked file carrying all four registered values; it is deliberately NOT committed (see the contract-test ticket above — committing turns `main` red). Good news measured today: **nothing auto-commits this repo** — no LaunchAgent invokes `git`, no hook or `.sh` runs `git add`, and the only `git commit` in `agents-sdk/` is `skill_optimizer.py`, which is manual-trigger-only and scoped to skill files. The Obsidian-Git vault owner was retired 2026-07-05. So the "auto-commit sweep" hazard is effectively nil and the value is durable at rest. **The real hazard is the next deploy**: if an incoming commit modifies `config.toml`, `--ff-only` will refuse rather than clobber (which is the safe failure), but whoever resolves it must re-apply the four registered values or the drill silently disarms mid-window. Decide before the next Mini deploy whether to (a) hold the registration as a documented local delta, (b) move `acknowledged_device` behind Keychain like the other Pushover values so the tracked config can stay unarmed and committed, or (c) accept committing `sean-phone` once the contract test is fixed. Note the privacy calculus is milder than the runbook assumes: a device NAME is a label, not an authenticator, and the repo already discloses Sean's identity comprehensively (git author, `/Users/seanwinslow/` paths, CLAUDE.md). — assigned: Sean

- **The Mini will silently drop off the tailnet after a reboot — `TailscaleStartOnLogin = 0`** (found 2026-08-30 while closing B11's first half). `defaults read io.tailscale.ipn.macsys TailscaleStartOnLogin` returns `0` on the machine that runs the fleet, so Tailscale is up right now only because it was launched by hand at 11:32. This is the same shape as the standing `RunAtLoad` gap in the fleet plists: a reliability mechanism that is installed but not durable, and whose failure is silent — after a power cut or an OS update the Mini leaves the tailnet, and ADR-02's answer page becomes unreachable with nothing announcing it. **Fix is one checkbox** (Tailscale → Settings → run on login), but it is a config change on a production host and was deliberately left for Sean rather than flipped by an agent. Worth deciding at the same time whether reachability deserves its own health check, since nothing currently notices the Mini being off the tailnet. — assigned: Sean
- **Tailscale's macOS GUI cannot add an account on the Mini — `Unable to add a new user. Please try again.`** (found 2026-08-30). Clicking *Sign in to your network* and *Sign up* in the desktop app did nothing; the Accounts pane errored. Everything underneath was provably healthy, so this is a bug in the app's OAuth handoff and not an install or network fault: the system extension read `[activated enabled]` (`io.tailscale.ipn.macsys.network-extension 1.102.3`), the daemon answered `Logged out.`, `login.tailscale.com` returned 302 with 27ms DNS and `derpmap/default` returned 200. **Workaround that worked, worth keeping** — bypass the GUI entirely with `/Applications/Tailscale.app/Contents/MacOS/Tailscale up --hostname=seans-mac-mini`, which prints an auth URL and returned `Success.` Reach for this again on the next device rather than re-fighting the dialog. - **Floors page check 15 (route-transition aperture) is flaky by ~5 ms** (surfaced 2026-08-29 during wayfinder #187, four consecutive `npm run floors` runs of identical `seanwinslow.com` code: two fully green, one red on check 15, one red on 15+19). The failure reads "the native pseudo-dim aperture did not land by 1200ms" with `elapsed=1205.2`, `apertureTime=1200`, `active=false` — the probe sampled 5 ms after the view-transition had already finished, so it measured a completed animation as an unlanded one. Nothing to do with the beats band (that leg is green on all seven routes at 1x and 4x). This is a gate that cries wolf: a flaky floor trains you to re-run instead of read, which is exactly how a real red gets waved through. Fix options: (a) sample against `apertureTime >= 1200` rather than requiring the transition still be `active`, (b) widen the window to the frame budget (~1216 ms), or (c) drive the probe off the animation's `finished` promise instead of a wall-clock sample. — assigned: Sean
- **`run-tests-on-stop.sh` silently backgrounds `npm test` on every session stop in exported projects** (surfaced 2026-08-28 during the hook audit). Inert in code-brain — the repo root has no `package.json`, `pytest.ini`, `setup.py`, `pyproject.toml` or `Makefile`, so every branch of its if/elif falls through — but `shared/hooks/` exports it to preset consumers, and in any Node project it fires `npm test >/dev/null 2>&1 &` on *every* Stop, unprompted and invisibly. It also never drains stdin (Stop hooks are piped JSON). Decide: gate it behind an opt-in env var, drop it from the exported presets, or keep it and document the behaviour in the installer README. Unlike the other eight hooks this one is not broken — it works, which is the problem. — assigned: Sean

- **require-confirm-highrisk.sh matches prose, not just command position** (surfaced 2026-08-28, immediately after the fix landed): the hook regex-matches the entire command string, heredoc bodies included, so a `git commit` whose *message* discusses a dangerous command is denied — it blocked its own fix commit for containing the word `mkfs` in the changelog prose. Workaround in use: write the message to a file and `git commit -F <file>`, so no trigger token appears on the command line. Proper fix options: (a) strip heredoc bodies and quoted string literals before matching, (b) require the token in command position (start of line or after `|`/`&&`/`;`), or (c) skip matching inside `git commit -m` arguments. Note the current behaviour errs toward blocking, which is the safe direction for a guard — this is a usability fix, not a security one. — assigned: Sean
- **A manual `--dry-run` meta-agent invocation silently overwrites the scheduled fleet-state.md** (diagnosed 2026-08-30; supersedes the 08-28 ticket). The launchd run does fire — the 08-30 08:45 run wrote real rows (indexer `chunks=184`, synthesizer `concepts=90 connections=27`, critic `codex_fail=1`), so the 08-28 all-placeholder snapshot was clobbered by the 11:05 manual dry run, not caused by a missed schedule. The diagnosis is closed; **the hazard is not fixed**: `--dry-run` still writes to the same production artifact the real run owns, so any manual invocation destroys that morning's fleet health record. Fix options: write dry-run output to a `-dry-run` suffixed path, refuse to write fleet-state.md at all under `--dry-run`, or refuse to overwrite a same-day file whose rows are non-placeholder. Note the alert-delivery log already took the third approach (dry runs write a self-identifying row rather than nothing) — fleet-state.md should match. — assigned: Sean

none states a live accepted risk), five tabled options with costs, the EXPLANATION.md link table, and Sean's objection that a repo link is a dig so linking is necessary but not sufficient. **Descriptions untouched** — all are `descriptionStatus: 'approved'` and several now under-sell what the panels say; needs Sean's ruling. **CORRECTION 2026-08-28 — my own measurement error, and the README fix that followed.** The earlier entry above (and `seanwinslow.com` commit `e3e2bb3`'s message) states the canonical SKILL.md *"scores 1/25 because its nine section headings sit inside code fences."* **Both the number and the cause were wrong.** The `1/25` was an artifact of my own tool misuse: `audit_intent_spec`'s `max_length` paginates the **input**, and I passed 3000/900/700 against a **31,915-character** file, so I graded a few hundred characters. Fences are irrelevant — a fenced-heading probe parses fine; frontmatter is irrelevant too. **Correct measurements, default settings:** current SKILL.md → **14/25, 2 anti-patterns**; the file as it stood 2026-05-04 (audit week) → **17/25, 1 anti-pattern**; both report `next_chunk_token`, so even a default call reads only 10,000 of ~32,000 chars. **The README claim still does not reproduce, but for a better reason than I first gave:** `23/25` was a **single paginated chunk reported as a whole-file score**, lifted from the 2026-05-08 Phase-3 verification whose own 30k round-trip table shows chunk 1 at 23/25 and chunks 2-4 at 1/25. **README + docs/EXPLANATION.md corrected and committed (`sw-mcp-intent-engineering` `d8ed73c`)**: measured result, date, one-line reproduction command, why the original was wrong, and the missing `--whole-file` aggregation named as a known gap rather than quietly shipped; CHANGELOG entry added under the repo's §9 docs-only rule. **projects.ts needs no change** — `118 skills` remains the right figure for that panel, since `23 of 25` still does not reproduce. **Still to verify (ticketed in the wayfinder prompt):** the README's 118-skill paragraph also claims *"zero parse errors"* and *"in under a second"*, neither re-measured, and `assess_retrofit_level` may paginate the same way. **PANEL-ART v2 IS OPEN (Sean, 2026-08-30, via wayfinder #190).** The 2026-08-27 stills-only ruling is lifted: a card may now carry a clip at `public/media/panels/<name>.mp4`, which `SceneFrame` picks up with no wiring. Two consequences for this pass: **every one of the ~30 cards now carries a motion question**, and **loop-seam standards are owed before the pass runs** (the existing plates already record seams — card 1 at 0.214/255, card 5 at 0.044/255 — so the bar exists but has never been written down as a gate). **Anima's panel 1 leaves this list**: it is now filled by the project's own rendered output (the Spark loop, `anima-spark-loop`), not authored art. One art-direction fact learned there and worth carrying: **the band panel box is 3.655 aspect and `object-fit: cover` crops hard**, which is why the existing Code Brain cards read as panoramas — a 16:9 composition loses ~49% of its height and clips anything near the top or bottom edge. Shipped Code Brain card art is **1120×630**, not the 1120×600 the payload rule in `projects.ts` states; the rule is wrong and should be corrected at the next edit.

Full evidence in `seanwinslow.com/docs/choreography/segerman-audit-2026-08-17/diff.md` §1–§2 and DESIGN §5.1 (commit `27e0eed`). **Ordered by evidence, cheapest and sharpest first.** (1) **Inner-page display scale** — one `calc(var(--fs-hero) * 0.52)` in `work/[slug].astro:100` and `info.astro:98`. Our home matches the reference to 3%; our About title is **3.95× smaller** than his and our project title **2.16×**. His token *grows* away from home and steps with the breakpoint; ours does neither. **Highest ratio in the audit, on a page Sean explicitly flagged.** (2) **The type-scale inversion** — one media query; at 1120px our hero renders at phone size (69.35px vs 69.22px at 390px). We copied his root rule on purpose, but his architecture switches at 1100px and ours does not, so we took the numbers without the thing that justified them. (3) **Route-transition duration** — 520ms vs his ~2.0–2.8s; tokens already exist, so it is one edit; §5 decided the mechanism and never the duration. (4) **The one-screen fixed shell** — genuinely architectural, do NOT decide in a hurry, but record it as open rather than leaving it invisible. **Then** text arrival beats, with the audit's §4 in hand: the reference does **not** do them in CSS (SplitText 3.13.0 driving GSAP against WebGL-composited Troika text inside a 2.6s router transition), so the zero-JS route is a *different, cheaper effect* rather than the same one for free. Home only; project and About are CSS grid and reflow. Root cause: the layout and the type scale disagree about where a band ends. Fix is one media query; the work is the floors check L26/1 never had. **Execute this first** — it is a live bug and its outcome is an input to spec 2. **Spec 1 — display scale + renames** ([spec](../../../seanwinslow.com/docs/plans/2026-08-17-display-scale-spec.md) · [plan](../../../seanwinslow.com/docs/plans/2026-08-17-display-scale-plan.md), commit `f206359`): `--fs-title-about` **1.15×** / `--fs-title-project` **0.85×**, named in tokens.css; (b) the audit's 'type-scale inversion' row was the **symptom**, the broken band was the cause, and the audit missed it because it measured type sizes and box widths and never asked whether two boxes overlap; - **segerman.dev FULL AUDIT — the north star, measured; blocks the paused text-beats decision** (2026-08-16) — **§B (experience measurement) DONE 2026-08-17**, commit `69b481a` in `seanwinslow.com`: `docs/choreography/segerman-audit-2026-08-17/experience.md` + 98MB of frames, both sites measured with one CDP rig at 390/768/1280/1920 in both themes; calibration gate passed (all 8 of our recorded numbers recovered, 520/170/150/230ms read off a live transition); **his project-page reveals captured for the first time** (306 frames @11.8ms, ~2.6s vs our 525ms); type scale inherited verbatim including BOTH inversions (760→768 −59.6%, 1080→1120 −40%, identical on both sites — at 1120px our hero is phone-sized); his display token goes 10.4→12→22rem across home/project/about where ours goes 10.7→5.56→5.56 (his About title 163px vs our 41px — this is the home/about difference Sean flagged); his dark mode is invisible to CSS and lives in WebGL; at ≥1100px his pages are one viewport tall and the DOM never scrolls. **Still open: §A (architecture archaeology, Codex) and §C (the diff + verdicts).** Original scope below.  Sean's call, in his words: *"I don't want to keep going down a rabbit hole of loop holes and workarounds if we have an end result to learn from and dissect at our fingertips."* **The real driver is his own observation** that he is *"noticing differences on our home page and about pages"* and wants to know they will *"sync up down the building pipeline"* — so this is a **diff**, not admiration. **Why now**: the site has been built against the reference through a keyhole — ONE screen recording of the intro scroll-up, and one analysis of that single moment, which itself records that there is **no footage of the project pages**; that absence is exactly why the route-transitions session could not judge sufficiency and had to ship on Sean's call. Everything else about the reference was *inferred from its shipped dependency list*. Doing this now costs one session; doing it after two more features costs this session plus rebuilding them. **Kickoff written: `seanwinslow.com/docs/prompts/2026-08-17-segerman-audit-kickoff.md`** (commit `f6d8199`). Three halves, deliberately matched to three different tools. **(A) Architecture archaeology** — library inventory with versions and *config constants* (the loader's catch-up 0.9 / ease floor 0.08 / min 2400ms are the model: find numbers, not names), barba route + persistence config, the velocity warp's uniforms and how Lenis drives `uSpeed`, payload per route, full page inventory. **Codex 5.6 SOL High is the right tool for this half** — patient long-context reading of minified bundles, and it keeps Claude usage for the half that needs a driven browser; repo precedent is `vault_critic` shelling out to Codex CLI at $0 incremental. Require file/line citations back into the bundles. **(B) Experience measurement** — layout box geometry, type (computed family/size/line-height/tracking/axes per role), colour, **timing read off `getAnimations()` rather than guessed from footage**, scroll choreography (ScrollTrigger start/end, what pins, what is scroll-linked vs time-based), and **the project-page reveals captured as stepped frames for the first time** — at 390/768/1280/1920, light and dark. **Claude Code driving real Chrome over CDP**, NOT the in-app browser pane, which reports `visibilityState==='hidden'` and would make every timing measurement meaningless. **(C) The diff** — three-column table (reference / ours / delta) per page type, and **every row must carry one of exactly three verdicts: deliberate** (we chose differently and DESIGN records why — e.g. DOM+video instead of WebGL meshes), **drift** (nobody chose it, and this is the category Sean is worried about), or **not yet built**. A difference with no verdict is an unfinished audit. **Gate before trusting any of it**: the measurement rig must first reproduce OUR OWN known numbers (`--vt-morph: 520ms`, the 107px display size, the 16+8 floors) — a rig that cannot get our documented answers right cannot be trusted on his. **Only Sean can supply**: what he *likes*. He walks the reference (project pages especially) and marks the moments — **Claude in Chrome** is the right tool for that since it drives his real browser — and recordings land in `docs/choreography/` (read-only bible). Ask him to point at moments, not to review numbers. **The line this does not cross**: measure structure/proportion/timing/behaviour; do NOT copy his source, shaders, assets, copy or artwork. The output is a spec in our own words and numbers. **Deliverables**: `docs/choreography/segerman-audit-2026-08-17/{architecture,experience,diff}.md` + `frames/`, a recommendation section **allowed to contradict DESIGN §5**, and §5 updated only where the audit contradicts it. If the reference does its text beats in CSS, that settles the paused decision. **DONE 2026-08-17 — all three sessions, commit `27e0eed`.** A (Codex 5.6 SOL High) read the shipped bundles with file/locator citations; B (fresh Claude Code) measured **both sites** in one Chrome over CDP and shipped a `verify.mjs` re-asserting every headline number against raw JSON; C joined them with a verdict on every row. **Verified before use**: ran B's verifier (all pass), confirmed our root-scale provenance (`tokens.css:18-21`), confirmed the 0.52 inner-title multiplier, checked we had not inherited his dead `minEase` (we had not). **Three corrections to the inputs**, incl. one to my own earlier lead — the type-scale inversion is NOT our defect, his site has the same two inversions at the same widths to the same percentages. **THE FOUR DECISIONS NOBODY EVER MADE** (all `drift`): (1) **inner-page display scale** — his display token GROWS away from home (home 10.4 → project 12 → about **22rem**), ours shrinks to one shared `calc(--fs-hero*0.52)`; at 1280 our home matches his to **3%**, project is **2.16×** smaller, About **3.95×** smaller, and the multiplier is a component-file comment absent from DESIGN entirely — largest ratio in the audit, on a page Sean named; (2) **the type-scale inversion** — we copied his root rule deliberately and credited it, but at 1100px HIS whole architecture switches and the inversion sits on that seam; ours has no seam, so at 1120px our hero renders at 69.35px, indistinguishable from its 69.22px at 390px; (3) **transition duration** — 520ms vs his ~2.0–2.8s in five beats; §5 decided the mechanism and never the duration; (4) **the one-screen shell** — every reference page at ≥1100px is exactly one viewport with `html/body` fixed, 60/60 elements pinned and a media reel that **loops infinitely and has no bottom**; ours is a 945–3276px document. Largest structural difference between the sites and DESIGN had never mentioned it. **§5 corrected**: ScrollTrigger is **NOT shipped** (only GSAP core's generic hooks name it — the 2026-08-15 dependency-list reading inferred it from behaviour); the '0.08 ease floor' is **algebraically dead**; Lenis confirmed as the velocity signal but with a desktop/touch split; barba's persistent-canvas rationale promoted from inference to confirmed. Pinned versions: GSAP+SplitText 3.13.0, Lenis 1.3.17, barba 2.10.3, Three r181, Astro 5.16.3. **New and consequential**: his page typography is rendered into **WebGL via Troika SDF text**, which is why `getAnimations()` returns 0 across all 26 of his cells and why every timing number for him is pixel-derived. **Where we are plainly better, by choice**: home **9,762 B vs his 1,838,648 B (188×)**, project **7,680 B zero-JS vs 3.2–4.4 MB (417–568×)**, we answer `prefers-color-scheme` and we survive a resize across 1100px — which **permanently breaks his page**. **Unjoined/unmeasured**: accent colour on both sides (B's detector failed), his declared route-change timing (no WAAPI object — read ~2.6s as **2.0–2.8s**), his other four project pages, any engine but Chrome 151.
- Council/discovery spend ledger records pre-flight reservation amounts as rows ($11.19 council-reserved vs $0.79 actual; $7.30 discovery-reserved vs $0.53 actual, 2026-08-16) — verify budget.py settle logic actually replaces reservations before trusting daily/monthly caps, else caps trip early — assigned: Claude
- C2 "Golden Loop" falsification pass before week-4 build start: 5-tool teardown (Braintrust, Langfuse datasets, promptfoo, Statsig, Freeplay) + optionally show C2 mock to 2-3 recent AI-PM hiring managers (prj-ai-pm-system-thinking-strategy)
- **WS3 text arrival beats — the pencil twist, and closing Firefox** (2026-08-17): Next and last item in DESIGN §5's build order before the optional velocity layer. Continuation prompt at `seanwinslow.com/docs/prompts/2026-08-17-text-arrival-beats-continuation.md` (commit `e10f540`). **The task turns on a measurement that re-opens §5's own wording.** The GSAP bullet justifies SplitText partly on *"GSAP arrives with the intro anyway"* — true on home, where it is a gated dynamic import for fresh capable visitors, and **false on a work page, which has never downloaded it**. Measured: `gsap.min.js` **28.4KB gzipped** + `SplitText.min.js` **3.7KB**, against a whole work page that is ~7KB gzipped and ships **zero external JS**. So the naive reading of §5 would put roughly four pages' worth of JavaScript onto the exact route whose transition was just built at zero JS. Not a reason to refuse — a reason the trade must be re-earned, with the **build-time-split alternative priced rather than assumed impossible** (Astro can emit the headline already split into spans, with CSS doing the animation and no runtime at all; `HandUnderline.astro` is existing in-repo precedent for a drawn stroke). **Firefox is now specified rather than vague** — see the route-transitions ticket above for the recon result; the fix is to make check 15 **engine-aware** over two real contracts (the morph where the engine has it, the instant-navigation floor where it does not), have it report which branch it ran, add `--firefox` to `floors.mjs`, and re-run the induced-fault proof afterwards so the Chrome branch still fails when the site is broken. **Hazards the prompt carries**: the handoff already moves `.home__left` on its own curve, so a write-on must compose with it rather than fight it; a repeat visitor never has an "as the intro settles" moment; a work-page title now arrives three different ways, one of them *inside* a view transition; floors check 1 asserts contiguous headline strings in the served HTML, which a per-character split would break — and that is the check working; and **the motion budget is FULL**, so this is a new trade against §5's ledger, not headroom. **Skills the prompt names**: brainstorming (required before the choreography), writing-plans, TDD, verification-before-completion, systematic-debugging; craft via impeccable, animation-vocabulary, 2d-animation-principles, micro-interaction-patterns, visual-polish-checklist, ux-design-guidelines, modern-web-design — and `gsap-scrolltrigger` **only if the zero-JS route loses the trade**, deliberately not loaded first. **§A DONE 2026-08-16 — Firefox is in the gate, commit `0dd6e9b`.** Check 15 was made **engine-aware rather than lenient**: two real contracts, exactly one per engine — the morph and matched pair where the engine has cross-document view transitions, and §5's floor where it does not (instant navigation to a **complete** page: destination matches the tile's `href`, exactly one hero plate, and **nothing left behind**). That last clause is the one worth having, because the failure the floor branch hunts is a *half* transition — an engine that begins what it cannot finish — which is invisible to "did the page change"; it is now asserted as `leftoverPseudos=0 activeVT=false`. **The branch is chosen by engine capability, never by what the run observed, and the check names the branch it took.** `PageRevealEvent` is a property of the browser, not evidence a transition would have run, which is exactly what makes it the right discriminator: a Chrome with the opt-in deleted still reports `supported=true`, still takes the strict branch, still fails — **proved by inducing that fault**, `verdict=BAD fails=5`, not by argument. `floors.mjs` gained `--firefox` (opened like `--safari`; Gecko has neither an occlusion flag nor a drivable debugging surface). **Green in three engines: Chrome 151 `run=f1rc7k`, Safari 26.5.2 `run=fxp577`, Firefox 153.0 `run=f1hvai`, each `ALL checks=16 bad=0 verdict=GREEN`**, CDP 8/0, `--selftest` still flips check 15, 72 unit tests, `astro check` 0 errors. **The honest remainder, now printed by the runner itself**: the CDP suite is Chrome-only (it is the only way to set `prefers-reduced-motion`, throttle a link or drop a GL context), so the verdict reads `page=green (Firefox)  cdp=green (Chrome)` rather than a bare `cdp=green` — **reduced motion is verified in Chrome and in no other engine**, and that gap now covers Firefox as well as Safari. **Still open: §B research gate onward** (the GSAP-vs-zero-JS trade with the measured numbers in front of Sean, the brainstorming-gated pencil choreography, both arrival moments, and the new coverage). **§B RESEARCH GATE DONE, AND THE DECISION IS PAUSED BY SEAN 2026-08-16 — commit `26bfe19` / `f6d8199`.** Research doc: `docs/plans/2026-08-17-text-arrival-beats-research.md`, every figure measured in-repo, sourced. **Provenance check first**: the inherited "28.4KB/3.7KB" named paths that do not exist (the minified UMD builds live under `dist/`); both figures re-derived correct at `gzip -9`, and both irrelevant because this site bundles through Vite. **What this project actually emits** (built a throwaway page importing gsap + gsap/SplitText exactly as the site would, walked the transitive chunk graph, deleted the probe): gsap **27,796 B** + SplitText **3,588 B** = **31,384 B gzip**, against a whole work page of **7,680 B with zero external JS** — **4.08×the entire page**. Tree-shaking buys ~500 B; GSAP registers plugins at runtime and does not shake. **The asymmetry is the finding**: "GSAP arrives with the intro anyway" is TRUE for home (3,588 B marginal) and FALSE for a work page (31,384 B, all of it) — and the intro gate is *fresh visitor*, so it is only true on a FIRST visit even on home. **SplitText solves a problem this headline does not have**: it is free/unrestricted/bundleable (Webflow, Apr 2025), `registerPlugin` is required specifically to survive tree-shaking, and the font-load race genuinely is solved by `autoSplit`+`onSplit` — but its value is re-splitting text that REFLOWS, and `AI Product<br />Manager` is two lines, three words, break hard-coded. **B3 measured, not reasoned** (probe animation injected from outside the site via CDP, driven through a real tile click): an unguarded beat starts immediately and burns **525 ms of itself under the crossfade**, most of it before the page is even opaque; and `:active-view-transition` matches from before `load` until `finished`, so **one CSS rule** — `animation-play-state: paused` — holds the beat at `t=0` and releases it exactly at handover, **zero JS**, degrading correctly by construction on deep links, in Firefox and under reduced motion (§C's three entry paths get one mechanism, not three). **Two floors are not neutral**: reduced motion is a global `*` sledgehammer so it covers a CSS beat FREE and does not touch a GSAP one (which would need its own `matchMedia` guard); and floors check 1 asserts contiguous headline strings, so a per-LINE split leaves it alone while per-word/per-char turn it red — the check working. **The pencil vocabulary is already deployed**: `.mark::after` draws left-to-right on `--ease-draw` at `--dur-draw:150ms` in pure CSS, and `HandUnderline` is an existing aria-hidden SVG stroke; handwriting-by-`stroke-dashoffset` is disqualified on live TEXT (letters must become paths, at which point they stop being text for selection/search/screen readers) but legal on that mark, which is what §2.1's exhaustive grammar demands. **PAUSED because Sean wants the reference to settle it** rather than choosing between inferences — see the segerman.dev audit ticket. The measurements stand whatever the audit finds; §D choreography was deliberately NOT decided (it is gated on brainstorming, and the motion budget is full).
- **WS3 intro round 2 — the resting chart was sub-pixel on every phone, focus escaped the cover, and three "green" checks were testing nothing** (2026-08-15): Coverage went 7→13 page checks and 6→7 CDP checks, all behind a new `npm run floors` (build → serve → drive → verdict, one command, replacing six manual steps and a `grep`). **Three real site defects.** (1) *The resting chart rendered sub-pixel on every phone.* DESIGN §5 already recorded a rendering failure — 0.90px lines at the 518×292 tile — and that 0.90 turned out to be plain arithmetic (5 plate-px × the 0.180 fit), so I wrote the arithmetic as a test that reproduces the recorded number and therefore predicts any viewport. It predicted 0.68px on an iPhone 15 and 0.65px on an SE — *thinner than the failure already ruled unacceptable*. The renderer floored only the moving vocabulary because the resting stroke had to match `settled.png`; that file was deleted last session and the parity contract is now a state contract, so the reason had expired. Floors extracted to `src/intro/floors.ts`, inert at fit 1 so still-parity holds by construction. Measured 1.60px on four device metrics and on Sean's real iPhone. (2) *Focus escaped the cover.* The armed state hides header, `<main>` and footer but left all three tabbable — a keyboard visitor could tab the whole site while looking at a loading cover. `inert` now, applied by the **inline pre-paint script** (doing it in the deferred module left a window the harness caught by sampling inside it), which required moving the `overlay` slot to last in the body so the footer exists by then. (3) *The loader narrated itself 101 times.* §9 asked for a progressbar **and** a live region; the markup gave both to one element whose value walks 101 steps. Split: progressbar carries the value with an `aria-label`, a hidden `role="status"` announces exactly twice, and its first message is the only discoverable form of the skip contract — which any key has always honoured and nothing ever mentioned. **Two WebKit gaps closed** that §5 had called unreachable: no-WebGL2 and context loss needed a *seam*, not a driver — the harness server injects the identical `getContext` patch CDP uses, and the parent is same-origin so it can call `WEBGL_lose_context` on the real canvas. Both green in Safari now. **Frame times exist**: the whole intro costs 1–3 dropped frames inside the first 230ms at renderer init, and the handoff — the moment the site is judged on — drops **zero** frames at 1×, 4× *and* 6× CPU; Sean's iPhone ran p50 17ms / p95 17ms across 645 frames. **A real phone ran it**, via a probe the harness server injects on `?probe` (Xcode is not installed so the planned iOS Simulator route was dead; the LAN + a real device is better evidence anyway). Suspects closed: `100vh`/`100svh` diverge by a real 108px but `vh` ≡ `lvh` so the panel can never peek, costing 154ms of a 900ms sweep that the cover's own drift covers; chart floors confirmed on-device; no unrequested context loss. **The round's standing lesson, now in §5: a harness can be green while testing nothing.** The bfcache check passed while never exercising bfcache (the harness server's own `no-store` disqualified the page — found only by making the check report Chrome's `notRestoredReasons` instead of guessing); one check asserted the *opposite* of its contract by navigating "back" to `?intro`, which is a standing instruction to replay; and two a11y assertions passed against a CSS variable name and a comment about the attribute being removed. All three caught by watching each check fail before trusting it pass. Commits `523b9f5` `3ad35d4` `5c8e213` `85c82b8` `2f8bf10` `b86e353` `1e4a699` `b3e7188`. **Open for Sean, both needing him and neither blocking:** (a) *the touch-skip suspect is still open* — a phone has run the intro end to end but nobody has tapped one, so whether a tap skips too readily and whether the consumed tap declines to fall through to a link are unmeasured; the probe is built and it is one URL. (b) *Composition on portrait phones is a taste call, not a defect* — the chart contain-fits to a 393×219 band, 26–33% of screen height with 15px bots, floating in the plate's painted starfield; measured 1.26× of headroom before any cast sprite would touch an edge, so a fit-to-content option exists and is one line, but it crops plate margin and is his call. Also still unverified and stated as such in §5: reduced motion in WebKit (needs the real OS setting) and Firefox entirely. **Sean's two calls, taken this session and recorded in §5 so neither reads as an open question later:** portrait composition stays AS IS (the fit-to-content option was measured and declined — the covering starfield makes it read as a small constellation in a large sky, which is the point), and the section-G feel items (unlit lamp, idle variants, headline text-arrival beat) were each offered and each declined — next is CSS view transitions, as the build order already said.

- **WS3 intro floors harness built and run — it found the intro DEAD in every production build** (2026-08-15): Task 12 closed, and it earned its keep on the first run. **Rewrote the spec before coding it**: three of the plan's five checks asserted an architecture retired by the cover pivot (a settled `<img>` on `/`, a `static` state, a `loading→settled` walk with no `tracing`), so implementing them verbatim would have produced a harness that passed while asserting a contract the site no longer has; corrected list with every assertion traced to the line it comes from at `seanwinslow.com/docs/plans/2026-08-15-intro-floors-harness-checks.md`, and the plan's stale list kept unedited under a warning because an unrecorded drift is what caused the 518px-tile session. **Two production-only defects found, both of which had passed 37 unit tests, `astro check`, a clean build and Sean's feel gate.** (1) *The intro never ran in production at all.* The CSS minifier reserialises `--intro-max-wait: 15000ms` as `15s` and `--intro-floor: 3500ms` as `3.5s`; `index.ts` read them with `parseFloat`, which drops the unit — so the 15-**second** asset deadline was a 15-**millisecond** one and every fresh visitor to the built site retreated to the bare page ~50ms in, with the intentionality floor at 3.5ms. Dev never minifies, so every check anyone had run was looking at a different build from the one a visitor gets. Fixed with a unit-aware `cssTimeMs` plus a test that bans `parseFloat`-on-`getPropertyValue` anywhere in `src`. (2) *A stalled chunk hung the cover forever over an invisible page.* The deadline covered the asset phase only; a chunk request answered by nothing hangs `import()` before that race is built, and `renderer.init` can stall behind it — held open, the cover sat at 0% with `<main>` still parked 100vh down, which is precisely the segerman.dev failure DESIGN §5 stakes the contract on. One watchdog now spans the whole pre-canvas pipeline and stands down when the rAF loop starts. **Verified**: `scripts/intro-check.html` 7/7 green in real Safari 26.5.2 AND real Chrome 151 (no-JS floor, decoded pixels, repeat visitor, fresh walk with 101 monotonic % steps, stuck-asset retreat at 15.0s with `sw-intro-seen` deliberately unset, cover-equals-viewport, no residual transform); `scripts/intro-cdp-checks.mjs` 6/6 green in Chrome — reduced motion, no-WebGL2, `WEBGL_lose_context`, Fast-3G (at 100% all 16 assets really are in), offline mid-load, stalled chunk. The plan called Steps 3–4 manual; CDP is HTTP + a WebSocket on localhost, so they are permission-free and repeatable instead. **The harness is proven able to fail**: `?selftest=1` falsifies six of seven checks, and checks 6 and 7 had their real faults induced at the site one at a time (cover moved back inside `<main>` → 771×508@25,572 in an 820×540 viewport, check 6 red and only check 6; `intro-armed` left out of `disarm()` → check 7 red on all three assertions). 43 tests green, `astro check` clean, build clean. Commits `b9383f7` `7dc2eee` `80520cf` `c8cbadb`. **Two things for Sean.** (a) *Behaviour change he should know about*: a genuinely slow visitor (Fast 3G) now gets the complete page at 15s instead of the intro at ~31s and is not marked seen, so the intro still waits for them next visit — that is what a 15s max wait means, and `--intro-max-wait` is the knob if he wants slow visitors to still get it. (b) *Raised as a note, then **actioned on Sean's call the same session** (`54edacc`)*: `public/intro/settled.png` was 4MB shipping in `dist` and fetched by nothing — excluded from the byte manifest by name, referenced only by comments and the compositor that writes it. Deleted; `dist` drops from 41M to 37M. The parity contract it stood for was never a pixel comparison against the file (the timeline test asserts the final STATE), `npm run settled` regenerates it from `cast.ts` on demand, and `build-byte-manifest.mjs` keeps skipping the name on purpose so a regenerated file can never enter the loader's byte budget. Re-verified after the rebuild: `dist/intro` holds `plate.webp` and the sprites only, the 16-file manifest is unchanged, 43 tests and `astro check` clean, floors harness still 7/7 green in Chrome 151. DESIGN §5 amended again to record it. **Still not verified, stated plainly in DESIGN §5**: the six CDP checks are Chrome-only (WebKit has no equivalent no-permissions driver, so reduced motion, no-WebGL2 and context loss are confirmed in Blink and reasoned in WebKit); Firefox untested; no touch device. **Next per DESIGN §5 build order**: CSS view transitions (`view-transition-name` morphing tile → work-page hero), which is also what delivers the project-page reveals Sean flagged on segerman.dev; the velocity layer stays last.
- **WS3 intro v3 SHIPPED — full-page cover + Act-2 rewrite; Sean's feel gate open, Tasks 12/13 remain** (2026-08-15): Diagnosed Sean's Act-2 verdict with instrumentation, not guesswork (dev-only `window.__intro` probe on `/dev/scrubber/`, renderer state sampled every 0.05–0.1s). **Two root causes.** (1) *Placement drift*: the intro shipped as a 518×292px hero card inside `.home__media`, so lines rendered at 0.90px, signal dots at 2.9px and arrival overshoot at ~1px — the whole Act-2 vocabulary sat below the perceptual floor, which is exactly why the trace "died off" while the hub halo read fine. DESIGN §5 never specified placement; its skip-strike→*headline arrival* grammar and segerman.dev loader parity both describe a page-owning cover, and the tile came from the build plan misreading a comment that said the intro "lands **above** this". Sean confirmed the original intent (full-page loading cover). (2) *The end-dump*: ten of twenty edges drew simultaneously in 0.24s after all motion stopped — the literal "cut to all agents connected". **Ratified by Sean and built** (commit `7ada2af`): full-viewport fixed cover that fades out on settle (static-hero state RETIRED — repeat/reduced-motion/no-WebGL get the page immediately; no-JS floor is the cover's default `display:none` + inline pre-paint gate); plate now CONTAINS with the ground still covering (cover-fit cropped most of the fleet off a portrait window); envelope 8.0→**8.5s** (Act 1 4.0→3.5s, freeze 0.6s, Act 2 3.4→**4.4s**); **the wake now reveals the whole network dormant** as a radial wave ordered by true distance from the hub, the trace lights paths bright over it, and the settle *converges* — nothing is added at the end, and selectivity is finally legible because there is something to be selective against; halos capped at 3 + hub idle (was 6 at once); hub pulses instead of sitting pinned at 1.0 for 3.35s; failure now reads (dot stops dead via new signal `to` truncation, real gap snaps open, tool-use flinches, hub spikes-then-dips, monitor's beacon flash moved here where it means something) and its payoff is bound to its cause via a new `heal` beat (was housekeeping at TOTAL−0.14); arrivals rebuilt on 2D principles (peak deformation 7%→20–55%, squash/stretch along+across the travel vector, anticipation, arcs, decaying follow-through, irregular cadence; new drop/rise/sprout styles). Renderer gained `lit`/`bold`/`gap`/`gapAt` + screen-space floors so an active path is bold at any viewport while the resting chart keeps its exact plate stroke. **36 tests green** (2 new), astro check clean, build clean, final-frame parity verified live (0 non-settled edges, 0 halos at 8.50). Plan: `seanwinslow.com/docs/choreography/motion-revision-2026-08-15.md`. **Feel gate PASSED 2026-08-15** ("looks AMAZING") with one note: the ending was abrupt. Fixed in `cc80fec` — a 380ms hold (the brief's "hold the completed system for a beat") then the cover lifts off the top edge over 940ms while `<main>` rises 56px behind it and the blocks stagger, chrome last; cover travels 781px to the page's 51px, and that ~15:1 parallax is what reads as depth. Two bugs surfaced building it: the cover was mounted inside `<main>`, which the handoff transforms — a transformed ancestor becomes the containing block for `position:fixed` descendants, so the cover sat 51px low (Base gained an `overlay` slot outside `<main>`); and `--intro-floor` was still 4000ms after ACT1_END moved to 3.5s, so the ratified 8.5s piece was quietly running 9.0s (token fixed, and a test now pins `--intro-floor` to ACT1_END inside DESIGN §5's range). 37 tests green. **Handoff then inverted again (`67e3a4e`)** after Sean compared it to segerman.dev: "it doesn't feel like the loader slides up, it feels like the home page scrolls in." Frame-analysed his screen recording (`docs/choreography/segerman.dev-website-inspo/`, 8fps across the transition; write-up at `docs/choreography/segerman-handoff-analysis-2026-08-15.md`). **The reference's loader never leaves — it stays put and is covered**, while the page arrives over it as an under-scaled panel that grows to full-bleed; v1 had the agency on the wrong object. Rebuilt: cover only drifts -7vh, `<main>` sweeps `translateY(100vh) scale(0.94)`→home over 900ms as an opaque panel above it, blocks carry additional travel+stagger inside the panel (hero moves ~957px of its own, which is what reads as "scrolling"), plus a DOM-transform approximation of the velocity warp (skewY on text/media, flattening before rest). **Deferred by design**: the true velocity-coupled warp and the project-page reveals — both need Lenis as the velocity signal (§5 pins ≥1.3.26, lands *with* the effect) and a DOM-vs-WebGL-mesh decision our architecture hasn't made; §5's build order already sequences them after CSS view transitions and they should stay there. **DESIGN §5 amendment DONE (`bbab30a`, Task 13 Step 2)** — §5 had drifted from lagging the code into contradicting it, so this went past the planned one ledger line: contract text kept intact (it is what Task 12's harness checks against), amendments added in the file's own dated-marker convention — the intro is a full-page cover (with the drift that produced the 518px tile recorded explicitly, since an unrecorded drift caused it); **the static-hero state is retired** (that sentence was actively wrong); the `--intro-floor` ↔ `ACT1_END` invariant; the 8.5s two-act envelope + Act 2 never scrubs; contain-vs-cover; the handoff; `?intro`; L20 fleet pick superseded by the 15-role pivot; a ledger row for the handoff plus "the intro's slot is spent, not reserved"; and the Lenis/velocity bullet updated with the frame analysis. Explicitly NOT claimed: the plan's wording would have had §5 assert the floors were "verified by the Task 12 harness" — it has not run, and §5 now says so. **Task 12 CLOSED 2026-08-15** — the floors harness was built and run; it found the intro dead in every production build. See the next ticket. Note: Astro ignores the preview harness's PORT and picks its own fallback (4322) — read `preview_logs` for the real port.

- **WS3 Safari check PASSED, and it OVERTURNED the documented 10-bit failure mode** (2026-08-14): Ran the ship-gate Safari pass in a real Safari **26.5.2** (`Version/26.5.2 Safari/605.1.15`, `navigator.vendor = Apple Computer, Inc.`), verified not the Chromium preview pane. **Result: 7/7 clips decode and play**, no media errors, all 1040x586, playback advancing, and canvas pixel spread 32-65 — i.e. real decoded frames, not a blank surface reporting itself as playing. **The fit transforms also measurably land in WebKit**: gradient correlation raw -> corrected, code-brain 48->82, driftgate 37->78, groundwork 50->76, anima 34->69, intent 25->64, agent-fleet 26->62, eval-suite 42->56. **THE CORRECTION: "Safari cannot play H.264 High 10" is FALSE.** That claim was recorded in this file and in the prompt kit, and was always flagged as inferred from a VideoToolbox refusal rather than tested. Now tested by rebuilding the exact defect from the raw Seedance source: an H.264 **High 10 / yuv420p10le** file **plays in Safari** (1040x586, spread 31), and so does the raw **HEVC Main 10** at 1920x1080 (spread 31). Also worth knowing: **`canPlayType` is not evidence** — Safari answered `"probably"` for High 8-bit, High 10 AND HEVC Main 10 alike; it parses a codec string and never attempts a decode. **The `-pix_fmt yuv420p` rule still stands**, on the narrower and honest ground that High 10 drops off the hardware-decode path and a page with several live clips should not burn CPU/battery on software decode when 8-bit is free. Check decoded PIXELS (canvas std-dev), never `readyState`. - **WS3 Groundwork semantic audit CLOSED; agent-fleet replaced; DESIGN.md trued up; motion-fit tooling moved into the repo** (2026-08-14): **Groundwork audit — the suspected accumulator is FALSIFIED.** Ran a per-pixel temporal activity map and a first-vs-last seam map over all 121 frames. The archive slot (stacked spheres, bottom-right foundation niche) has **zero activity** — nothing ever arrives there, so it cannot accumulate; the concern in the handoff was hypothetical and the drawing simply never animates that region. **The whole foundation is loop-safe**: only the gear rosettes (rotation, a perfect cycle) and the central drafting-ink column (accent bloom, pulsing) move below the ground line, and **0% of the seam is below ground**. **The real seam is 100% above ground**, on the four horizontal crate lanes and the two ramps (left helix, right chute): the crates advance but do not complete a whole number of pitches in 120 frames, so the closing frame is offset from the opening one. That is the even-spacing-advance rule failing, NOT an accumulator. Two secondary notes: the third floor mixes travelling crates with static round-headed fixtures sitting in the lane (hazard #2, reads fine because they are clearly background machinery), and **the lanes do not run off either edge of the frame**, so §7.1's closed-circuit escape hatch is unavailable and the loop can only close by exact pitch. **Verdict: leave it.** The mismatch is spread across dozens of small crates rather than concentrated in one high-contrast element, which is why 3.51 reads as faint shimmer rather than a cut; the composition is strong and a re-roll risks more than it buys. If ever re-rolled, the prompt needs an explicit advance-by-exactly-one-crate-spacing clause plus a hold on the round-headed fixtures. **Agent Fleet replaced**: Sean's brighter Photoshop redraw + v3 clip. Raw download was HEVC **Main 10 / yuv420p10le** — re-encoded to the kit's spec (h264 High, yuv420p, crf 20, 1040 wide, no audio). Loop seam 5.06 (was 2.48), mid-pack against anima 4.68 / intent 4.75; bloom pulses as an oscillator and the composition holds, so accepted. Old still + clip archived to `src/assets/motion-archive/*-superseded-2026-08-14-v2.*`. **DESIGN.md trued up**: new **§3.2** records Sean's call that no night grade ships (with the mechanism preserved so it is not re-derived: lit windows / moon / lantern are bare cream paper, identical in value to the margin, so no global tone curve can separate light from paper and inversion must turn every light into a hole); **§5 motion budget restated** as a spend ledger, recording that the scrapped cursor trail's slot was spent on tile hover motion rather than banked, and flagging that Week 2's four candidates are unbudgeted — with the note that Lenis and route transitions change navigation rather than decorate it, so their trade is against the intro's job, not against the marks; **§9** stale "red underline" fixed. Same stale-red comments fixed in `src/styles/tokens.css` §8 and `src/styles/base.css`. **New repo tooling**: `scripts/measure-motion-fit.py` (self-contained uv script) replaces the scratchpad chain — run it after ANY clip re-roll. It flags `slack < 0` (transform would expose an edge) and `drift > 0.6%` (framing MOVES during playback, which no static transform can fix). **It found one: eval-suite drifts 1.80%** against a 0.00-0.45% floor everywhere else, which is why its fit tops out at 0.681 while the other six reach 0.78-0.94. That tile keeps a small residual shimmer on hover; accepted, since re-rolls stopped improving it. **Also caught a bug in my own tool**: the first `drift` metric compared only horizontal translation, so a pure push-in would have reported a clean 0.00% — exactly the defect it exists to catch. Now takes the worst spread across all four parameters. **Anima push-in: not present.** Measured drift 0.20%, about 1px at tile size, i.e. the measurement floor. Nothing to chase; Sean's read that the image and video look great as-is is correct.
- **WS3 hover jump diagnosed and fixed in two passes; Higgsfield CLI was logged out** (2026-08-14): Sean reported every tile's art shifting up-and-slightly-left and changing size on hover. **Cause: Seedance does not return the frame it was seeded with.** Pass 1 — every clip comes back framed 1-3% wider and 1.5-3.5% LOWER than its seed, vertical offset 2-3x the horizontal, which is exactly the "up and slightly left" Sean saw. `SceneFrame.astro` had asserted the 220ms crossfade hid this; it did not, and a crossfade across a misalignment is a morph, which reads worse than a cut. The offset is CONSTANT within a clip (5 of 7 measured zero movement first-to-last frame at a 0.25% grid), which is what makes a static per-scene fix legal. **The naive fix fails**: several scenes need 3.4% of downward shift but only 1.4% of upscale, so shoving the clip down walks it off its own top edge and opens a gap — neither field of view contains the other. Fix zooms BOTH still and clip onto the field they share. Pass 2 — a residual remained because pass 1 used a single uniform scale and **the clip is also SQUEEZED**; Sean caught it by eye as "slightly more compressed and squished together". Measured, it is systematic: every clip needs **1.45%-1.71% more vertical scale than horizontal** (~8px in a 520px tile). Source is the resolution chain — drawings are 1344x752 (1.7872) or 1672x941 (1.7768), Seedance renders 1920x1080 (1.7778), encode lands at 1040x586 (1.7747); nothing in it preserves the seed's proportions. **Only the CLIP takes the anisotropic correction**; the still is zoomed and cropped but NEVER stretched. Gradient correlation at 320px, raw / uniform / +aspect: code-brain .341/.774/.878, groundwork .360/.719/.826, driftgate .241/.748/.812, anima .215/.732/.777, agent-fleet .195/.635/.752, intent .180/.687/.689, eval-suite .298/.509/.538. Correcting aspect also SHRANK the crop (worst 2.80% -> 2.16%; four scenes now under 0.2%) because a better-fitting clip overlaps the drawing more. Files: `src/data/motion-fit.ts` (new, carries numbers + reasoning) and `src/components/SceneFrame.astro`. The still is only cropped where a clip can actually be reached, so reduced-motion / touch / no-hover show the drawing whole. **Follow-ups: (1)** any re-rolled clip invalidates its `motion-fit.ts` entry and must be re-measured — eval-suite's is knowingly stale the moment Sean's web-app regen lands. Scripts in order: `align/register_cover.py` -> `align/register_aniso.py` -> `align/fit_aniso.py` in the session scratchpad. **(2)** `higgsfield` CLI was logged out (eval v6 died 19:10 with `Not authenticated`); Sean has re-authenticated. **(3)** a long-running `npm run dev` serves STALE cached image transforms — the :4321 server predating commit baddd0f was still showing the three RETIRED concepts (gate, checkpoint, open film strip) while disk held the new ones. Restart the dev server after replacing any scene asset, or review against `npm run build`.

- **WS3 media ops: three failure modes that cost hours, all now documented** (2026-08-14): (1) **`higgsfield generate list` only returns `completed` jobs** — an in-progress job is absent from it. Reading that absence as "the job was dropped" sent a whole diagnostic down the wrong path; use `generate get <id>`, and `generate create` *without* `--wait` to get the id immediately. (2) **Render times vary 4x by scene** — most clips finish in 6–7 min, Code Brain takes ~26, Eval Suite has run 39+. A short client timeout is indistinguishable from a failure. Use `--wait-timeout 55m`. (3) **Passing a local path to `--start-image` re-uploads the file every submission**, and Higgsfield runs an IP check on new media; referencing it before the check clears refuses the job — the web app says *"IP check not finished for input media"*, the CLI surfaces a bare **HTTP 502** with no explanation. **Fix: `higgsfield upload create` once, wait ~2 min, reuse the UUID.** Sean found this one from the web app UI after the CLI gave nothing useful. Also: **encode must pass `-pix_fmt yuv420p`** — Seedance returns 10-bit and the earlier encodes carried it to H.264 High 10, which VideoToolbox refuses and Safari cannot play (all six re-encoded from raws; unverified in a real Safari, worth one manual check before ship).

The real signature is simpler and stranger: **code-brain video jobs are created normally, enter `in_progress`, and stay there far past the ~6-7 minutes all six sibling clips took.** The original `fe59c3fd` eventually resolved to `failed`; job `bc4d1646` sat `in_progress` for 11+ minutes under direct polling. A two-arm diagnostic was run to discriminate: **Arm A** same seed with the glow-breathing and star-twinkling clauses removed (testing whether many small luminance oscillations over a dark field were the cause), **Arm B** original prompt verbatim with the seed downscaled 3.1MB PNG -> 270KB JPEG (testing whether the largest-of-seven seed was the cause). **Both arms behaved identically to the untouched attempts, so both hypotheses are falsified** — it is neither the prompt's luminance clauses nor the seed payload. That is a description, not a diagnosis. **Do not spend more on this without a new idea** — three renders are already sunk. Site is unaffected: six of seven tiles animate and `SceneFrame` treats a still-only tile as a complete designed state.

Seams — 2.5 i2v **6.64**, 2.5 t2v **6.29**, 2.0 t2v **7.80**. The premise was that a model authoring its own frame zero would loop far better; it bought **0.35 of a seam point**, and the 2.0 arm was actually worse than the seeded control. Cost was severe and landed on locked rules: Seedance 2.0 drew a **ruled construction grid** across the paper (DESIGN §3 bans ruled lines outright) in a generic explainer-video register that §1's anti-template rule exists to keep off the site; Seedance 2.5 held the pencil far better but cropped to a macro shot that threw away the composition, dropped the caricature register to plain smiley robots, and turned the **entire drawing** drafting-ink blue, past even the relaxed §2.1 reading. **The real finding: loop quality is a PROMPTING property, not a seeding property.** The seeded regen moved 13.29 → 6.64 on prompt changes alone, a bigger swing than any model or mode choice produced. Do not re-open t2v for the tile set. Review console preserved at `ws3/review/index.html` in the session scratchpad; three-way clips are side by side and looping there.
- **WS3 shipped clips were 10-bit H.264 — fixed, but check any future encode** (2026-08-14): All six tile clips shipped as H.264 **High 10** / `yuv420p10le`. Seedance returns 10-bit and the earlier encode command had no `-pix_fmt`, so ffmpeg carried it through. VideoToolbox refused to hardware-decode them and fell back to the software decoder — VideoToolbox is the path Safari depends on for H.264, and Safari has no High 10 support, so these were plausibly dead tiles in Safari. All six re-encoded 8-bit from the original raws at the same crf 20 / 1040 wide / no audio (no double resample). **Any future tile encode must pass `-pix_fmt yuv420p -profile:v high`.** Unverified tail: this was not confirmed in a real Safari, only inferred from the VideoToolbox refusal plus documented High 10 support — worth one manual Safari hover before ship.

Not a bug and not a blocker — clips are `preload="none"` and load one at a time on first hover — but it is a real size-versus-motion call Sean has not made. Options if it matters: accept, or cut the station count in a future regen. Do not silently raise crf; 20 is locked after the crf 31 disaster.

- ⚠️ **PRIVACY LEAK — remediated 2026-08-11; the last step is FILED and waiting on GitHub.** *(Updated 2026-08-12: GitHub Support ticket **#4659070** submitted from Sean's account, status open. Routing note for any refile: do NOT use Repositories → Deletes, that is a repository-deletion flow; this went in under Repositories → Repository features → Branches. Also verified: `code-brain` has **0 forks / network 0**, so GitHub's fork caveat does not apply and the remedy can fully close the hole. Gate closes when a fresh `git clone --mirror` comes back clean, not when Support replies.)* The `git filter-repo` scrub ran and was force-pushed. **A normal `git clone` is now completely clean** (0 manifest objects, 0 `ats:` slugs, 0 bucket names, verified against a fresh clone from GitHub, 781 commits intact). **But `git clone --mirror` still yields the data**, because GitHub retains `refs/pull/*` forever and 140 PR refs carry the pre-rewrite commits in their ancestry — 104 manifest objects still reachable by object SHA (e.g. via `refs/pull/141/head`). No PR *tip* contains them, so nothing shows in a PR diff view, but anyone who runs `git clone --mirror` + `git rev-list --objects --all` gets the 69 company names. **The only remedy is GitHub Support:** open a ticket from Sean's account asking them to garbage-collect stale refs and purge cached views for `seanwinslow28/code-brain` — this is GitHub's own documented procedure after a sensitive-data force-push, and it cannot be done from the client side (PR refs are not deletable via push, and closing/deleting PRs does not remove them). Nuclear alternative: delete and recreate the repo, which is immediate and total but destroys 140 PRs, the issue tracker (`docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` names GitHub Issues as the project tracker), stars and forks. **Backup:** full pre-rewrite history preserved and verified at `~/Code-Brain/code-brain-git-backup-2026-08-11.bundle` (1.1 GB, `git bundle verify` passed) — keep it until Support confirms, then delete it, since it contains the unredacted data.

The Mini is **1 ahead, 0 behind, working tree clean**, so it is a plain fast-forward whenever Sean is at the machine or a credential helper / SSH remote is configured. Consider switching the Mini's remote from HTTPS to SSH so headless pushes stop depending on an unlocked Keychain.
- **OPERATIONAL LESSON — `reset --hard` after a history scrub deletes the scrubbed files from disk** (learned the hard way 2026-08-11, near-miss): resetting the Mini to the rewritten branch removed all **92 job-feed manifests from the working tree**, because filter-repo had removed them from history and the reset faithfully reproduced that tree. Those manifests are the entire evidence base for Owner's Card 2 (158 refusals / 235 runs / 92 days / 488,461 fetches) and **20 of them existed only on the Mini** — the MBP mirror stops at 2026-07-22. Recovered in full from the pre-reset commit `ae803a2`, which survived in the object store (`git show ae803a2:<path> > <path>` per file); verified by re-deriving 235 runs / 158 refusals exactly, and they now sit untracked-and-ignored, which is the correct end state. **The generalizable rule: before resetting any clone onto scrubbed history, copy the scrubbed paths out of the working tree first** — the pre-sync backup taken from `git status --porcelain` did *not* include them, because they were tracked and unmodified and so never appeared as dirty. A backup keyed on "what git says changed" misses exactly the files a history rewrite is about to delete.
