[Move A verification](../20_projects/substack-studio/research/deep-dives/2026-08-05-move-a-verification-two-named-examples.md) then checked those two directly: **Techpresso FALSIFIED** (50 copy-paste prompts, zero eval results, zero documented failures) and **Aksoy Capital's 1-hour retraction policy is real and accurately reported but unexercised** (`/data/retracted/`, `/retractions/`, `/corrections/` all 404, no searchable instance) **and out-of-domain** (algorithmic financial research, not a technique library). Net: the white space is REAL, but the claim must be stated precisely — "no prompt or technique library publishes per-entry tested verdicts with honest failures", NOT the broad "nobody does this", which Aksoy would falsify. Methodological lesson worth its own post: query SHAPE drove source tier (research-shaped → 88% academic, market-shaped → 65% marketing, same model/tier/day), and the bad sources were fresh 2026 marketing, not stale research, so recency filters cannot catch them. **✅ KICKOFF E COMPLETE 2026-08-05 ($0, both moves).** [Move B](../20_projects/substack-studio/research/deep-dives/2026-08-05-move-b-candidate-falsification.md) checked twelve named candidates by direct fetch (URL behind every yes AND every no) plus an Executive Circle MCP pass. **CORRECTION 2026-08-05: that pass does NOT discharge refocus item 2 in full — an earlier version of this line said it did, and that was wrong.** Move B queried Executive Circle on the *library-model* question only, which is [Kickoff C](../20_projects/substack-studio/continuation-prompts/KICKOFF-2026-08-05-C-competitive-checks.md) **step 3** of five. Kickoff C's actual body — per-territory Nate-Jones + web competitive checks across all SIX territories, each ending in a CLEAR / CROWDED-BUT-DIFFERENTIATED / COMMODITY verdict, output at `research/2026-08-05-competitive-check-six-territories.md` — is UNRUN; Move B's note contains zero mentions of any of the six territories and that output file does not exist. Eleven clean NOs — Anthropic prompt library, DAIR.AI, promptingguide.ai, LangSmith Hub ("user-generated and unverified", their words), PromptHub, PromptLayer, Braintrust, DSPy, OpenAI Cookbook, awesome-chatgpt-prompts, HF finephrase. **The twelfth changes the positioning: Nate Jones already publishes tested verdicts with honest failures** (2026-07-15 harness-audit post — 742-word brief vs 5,197-word method, 3 runs each, 2 blind scorers, his richer artifact scored 19.67/20 on content and FAILED the delivery contract 2 runs of 3, published as a loss with explicit scope limits). So "we test and admit failures" is dead as a differentiator; the surviving white space is **CUMULATION** — per-entry, versioned, retractable, against a published measurement protocol. Nobody does that. This upgrades Finding 8 from prudent to load-bearing: the measurement protocol IS the differentiation, not just its credibility gate. [Move C](../20_projects/substack-studio/research/deep-dives/2026-08-05-divergence-mechanisms-evidence-map.md) resolved all six publishable figures to primaries — all real and quoted accurately, with three corrections that must travel: (1) **d=0.414 does NOT support the masthead re-cut** — it rests on 2 studies / 6 effect sizes, the during-vs-after moderator test was NULL (p=.141, authors: "offers no evidence for a difference"), and they hedge with "might persist"; ship it with the n attached, demote it to open question, or make measuring persistence a rung; (2) the 91%/82% debate figure is Hegazy 2024 (arXiv:2410.12853) on **2024-era models**, and neither DR-cited source contains it; (3) NoveltyBench D=16.50 vs 13.60 belongs to continuous context sampling vs the G2 baseline (ACL 2026 SRW), **not to xRAG** — that paper calls xRAG's goal "orthogonal to ours". iDesignGPT's +18.5% / +11.4% / d≈1.03 / 48 participants confirmed verbatim in Nature Communications; all eight named frameworks confirmed real. ⚠️ **Doc re-anchor ticket is STILL BLOCKED** (an earlier version of this line declared it unblocked — corrected 2026-08-05). Item 3 of the research round is complete and item 2 is partially complete, but **two of the four original kickoffs have never run**: [Kickoff B — discovery pain-mining](../20_projects/substack-studio/continuation-prompts/KICKOFF-2026-08-05-B-discovery-pain-mining.md) (`fusion-discovery-council --lens substack` on the six territories' pain hypotheses; deep tier $4.00 recommended, needs Sean's spend confirm; latest discovery session on disk is 2026-07-30 and all ledgers predate the 2026-08-04 refocus, so **no territory pain hypothesis has any evidence behind it**) and [Kickoff C — competitive checks](../20_projects/substack-studio/continuation-prompts/KICKOFF-2026-08-05-C-competitive-checks.md) steps 1-2 and 4-5 ($0) — **Kickoff C is now ✅ COMPLETE (2026-08-05, see below); Kickoff B is the only kickoff still unrun.** This matters for the value gate: the DR round proved the white space is real and the mechanisms are sound, but neither question touches **whether readers actually have the pain** — the Itch slot that project CLAUDE.md §3 hard-blocks on. When those land, reconvene with the sidecar BEFORE the doc re-anchor, because Findings 1-2 and 7 all changed and the reconvene should decide the new positioning (cumulation, not testing) and what carries the masthead now. Nothing final until real posts prove the value. **✅ KICKOFF C COMPLETE 2026-08-05 ($0, all five steps)** — [competitive check on the six territories](../20_projects/substack-studio/research/2026-08-05-competitive-check-six-territories.md): per territory, the closest Nate Jones coverage (posts read in full, not previews) plus the 2-3 closest free equivalents, each fetched directly. Verdicts: **d1 Cartography COMMODITY as a beat / CLEAR as an instrument** (Wikipedia's WikiProject AI Cleanup already ships a free 50+-pattern attractor field guide built from thousands of real diffs — un-out-catalogueable by one person; this independently ratifies the critic's demotion of d1 from beat to instrument), **d2 Stolen Methods CROWDED-BUT-DIFFERENTIATED** (Zoe Scaman already feeds AI "frameworks from jazz improvisation, ecosystem design, evolutionary biology, network theory, game theory" with zero measurement and nothing shipped; six of Nate's eight adversarial prompts are themselves offline-protocol ports, stated and never run), **d3 Import Duty CROWDED-BUT-DIFFERENTIATED but weakest** ("constraint-based prompting" is saturated SEO advice; only the *social/physical* imports and the customs-violation report are open — if a draft's takeaway is "add constraints," kill it), **d4 Input Axis COMMODITY on the corpus half / CLEAR on the data-hole probe** (the most saturated shelf checked, and the closest surviving relative of the premise the GPT Image 2 test killed), **d5 Manufactured Opposition CROWDED-BUT-DIFFERENTIATED with the sharpest delta** (four free tools exist — `agent-review-panel`, `devils-advocate-agent`, `adversarial-workflow`, OpenClaw's suite — and not one installs a *stake* or tests whether the disagreement changed the output; all ship the *homogeneous* single-model variant the DR round's Finding 4 says underperforms, while Sean already runs the heterogeneous one), **d6 Sourdough CLEAR** (the memory field measures recall accuracy, not whether an aged creative collaborator beats a fresh one — but CLEAR partly *because* it is expensive; the absence may be a rational market response). **The finding that outranks all six:** free equivalents cover the MECHANISM in five of six territories and publish an EVALUATION of it in zero — `agent-review-panel`'s own README says it plainly: *"this project has not independently benchmarked review quality."* The commodity trap is one layer up from where the sidecar assumed: a post whose deliverable is a mechanism competes with GitHub; a post whose deliverable is a verdict on a mechanism competes with nobody. **Step 3 extended Move B into the category Move B never checked** — agent-skill marketplaces, the shelf the both-tiers rule would actually ship onto: claudemarketplaces.com indexes 23,600+ Claude Code skills carrying name/author/description/**install count only**, no test results, no verdicts, no failure cases (popularity standing in for evidence, the same trust substitution Finding 2 found in prompt marketplaces, one layer up). So the absence is now verified in four categories: prompt libraries, enterprise eval tooling, practitioner newsletters, agent-skill marketplaces. **Two findings outside the six:** (1) the folklore rung has partial encroachment — a free Medium post (2026-03) already published 20 runs across four temperature settings concluding "19 of 20 outputs recommended identical core strategies regardless of temperature," plus a free live tool, so the finding is no longer novel and the rung must be sold on protocol and scale, not surprise; (2) Nate's tactical artifacts are **paywalled** (both posts checked are truncated at the paywall), so he is an encroachment risk on *angle*, not a free equivalent on *artifact* — the genuinely free equivalents are GitHub repos, Wikipedia, and SEO content. Third independent pass now lands on the same build-order inversion: **the measurement protocol is the only item on the list a competitor is not already giving away.** Evidence only; the reconvene makes the keep/kill calls. **✅ KICKOFF B COMPLETE 2026-08-05 ($23.04 actual / $25.87 reserved against the $30/day policy-v3 discovery cap — note CLAUDE.md and the skill doc both still say $10/day $50/month, which is STALE; `council/cap_policy.json` v3 reads daily 30.00 / monthly 100.00 and is the enforced policy). Sean chose six narrow `standard` runs (one per territory) over the kickoff's suggested single `deep` run, because per-territory verdicts need per-territory framing. Synthesis at [research/discovery/2026-08-05-territory-pain-validation.md](../20_projects/substack-studio/research/discovery/2026-08-05-territory-pain-validation.md); six ledgers + six briefs alongside it; $0 agent backfill appended to every ledger and 13/13 quotes confirmed verbatim by `verify_supplement` (exit 0 on all six). **Verdicts: T1 Stolen Methods KEEP with the claim inverted** (forum pain says prompt *tips* fail; arXiv 2602.20408 says structured protocols — CoT + ordinary personas — produce "the highest idea diversity, outperforming humans", so the tips-vs-protocols gap IS the thesis and is now citable); **T2 Import Duty KILL as a demand claim, keep as story engine** (perfect 1.00 citation precision and still zero first-person evidence — the panel's own gap says the evidence "relies on conceptual articles"; the one real user quote is about *reliability* delaying a project, not constraints); **T3 Input Axis RESHAPE + RE-RUN, do not cite this ledger** (0.57 precision / 0.00 recall, the only Sonar-fed run of the six, five of six "independent domains" are consultant SEO squatting the "why AI writing sounds generic" keyword, and one citation credits a CAPTCHA-walled North Devon council complaints page with prose it cannot contain); **T4 Manufactured Opposition KEEP reshaped to theater-detection** (pain real — live r/claude thread — but three competitors already ship devil's-advocate prompts and NONE tests whether the output changed; even arXiv 2503.14263 measured satisfaction and psychological safety, not decision quality); **T5 Cartography RESHAPE, premise falsified** (the "nobody has mapped the attractors" half is plainly false — seanjkernan, michellekassorla, stationx and Reddit all ship tell-lists — but nobody tests accuracy, and arXiv 2502.15666 (APT-Eval, 14.7K samples, 12 detectors) finds detectors "frequently flag even minimally polished text as AI-generated", causing "false plagiarism accusations"; also note the evidence is writing-only, nothing on visual tells); **T6 Sourdough PROMOTE, strongest evidence in the round** (1.00 precision AND 1.00 recall, plus arXiv 2606.10949 — Writer, ICLR 2026 workshop, benchmarking Mem0/MemOS/Zep — "memory amplifies sycophantic behavior across all conditions, with up to 25x higher sycophancy rates than in-context baselines" and "every model triples its sycophancy rate under at least one memory system"; the sidecar's guess was right and its confidence was too low — though "nobody publishes longitudinal results" is now HALF FALSE, benchmarks exist, and the surviving white space is narrower: no PERSONAL longitudinal collaborator log published against a fresh instance). **The cross-cutting finding: three of six territories (T1, T4, T5) independently converge on "the diagnosis is commodity, the tested verdict is vacant"** — the same seam the DR round reached by a completely different method, and now a third independent pass at the same build-order inversion (measurement protocol before rung #1). **Five unpredicted pains logged:** fragility of centralized memory state (server dies, months of context die), dependence/autonomy degradation ("going back to a fresh session feels broken"), being FALSELY accused of AI as its own distinct pain, reliability-not-constraint-blindness as what actually breaks deadlines, and the privacy cost of feeding a personal corpus to a hosted model (absent from all six panels; the first objection a professional reader raises). **Method finding worth acting on:** `citation_precision` is recorded only in the session JSON, never rendered in the ledger, and it ranged 0.57–1.00 across one batch while every run reported "dropped: 0" — read it before trusting any ledger; and the `Corroboration: N domains` badge is domain-blind, so vendor SEO wears a "well-corroborated" label. Reconvene with the sidecar now — item 1 of the research round is closed and all four kickoffs have run. — assigned: Sean

Suite stays hermetic: no test reaches the network (0.45s wall, `genai` patched + `Client.assert_not_called()` retained). Only spotted because the body opened mid-Part-3. `interaction.output_text` is **not** a whole-report aggregate either (it equalled the final chunk alone), so it is now a degraded last resort, never a peer source. Fixed to concatenate all model_output steps; re-fetched the stored interaction (free — retrieval, not a re-run) and repaired the note in place, 27737 → 46167 chars, all of Deliverable A + Parts 1-5 + Conclusion now present, 60 citations. Two regression tests added from the measured shape (`test_extract_concatenates_every_model_output_step`, `test_extract_does_not_prefer_output_text_over_steps`). Lesson worth keeping: for this API, silent truncation is the default failure mode and it looks like success — assert on report structure, not just on non-emptiness. Commits `c04b057` + follow-up.

Intermittent (Phase 1 run #2 succeeded when fast). Fix: strip leading `: ` comment lines / extract the first balanced `{…}` before json.loads, harden `_parse`. ALSO: failed Fusion calls bill OpenRouter but record $0 locally (`record_spend` is post-success only in [__main__.py](tools/llm-council/council/discovery/__main__.py)) — record usage.cost on failure too. - discovery GATHER-stage spend invariant (Phase-3 review follow-up, LOW): the new `except DiscoveryFailed` cost-recording path in [tools/llm-council/council/discovery/__main__.py](tools/llm-council/council/discovery/__main__.py) only covers FUSE-stage spend; the generic `except Exception` after it assumes "no spend incurred pre-fuse." That holds today because FUSE (OpenRouter) is the only billable stage, but GATHER calls Perplexity Sonar + web search — if a Sonar/web call is ever instrumented to bill (or already incurs cost not surfaced as an exception attribute), a gather-stage failure would silently record $0, re-opening the exact cost-integrity bug Phase 3 closed one stage upstream. Flagged by the Task-3 code-quality review. Fix when GATHER billing is confirmed: thread gather-stage incurred cost into a typed failure the same way `FusionError.cost` → `DiscoveryFailed.cost_usd` does. Until then it's a documented invariant, not a live bug. — assigned: Sean
- ✅ DONE 2026-06-28 (Step-A hygiene, branch `feat/discovery-step-a-hygiene`): `_normalize_segment` in [pipeline.py](tools/llm-council/council/discovery/pipeline.py) now strips `:`/parens + collapses whitespace (incl. whitespace-only → "") once at the `run_discovery` boundary, so every collector gets the cleaned segment. ORIGINAL — discovery `--segment` free-text into operator-bearing queries (Phase-5 follow-up, LOW): the Phase-5 `--segment` qualifier composes raw free text into the github (`f"{subject} in:title,body is:issue"`, [tools/llm-council/council/discovery/gather/github.py](tools/llm-council/council/discovery/gather/github.py)) and reviews (`({weak}) ({sites})` grouping, [tools/llm-council/council/discovery/gather/reviews.py](tools/llm-council/council/discovery/gather/reviews.py)) queries unescaped. A `--segment` carrying operator tokens (`is:pr`, an unbalanced `)`, `site:foo`) alters query semantics rather than just narrowing the audience — a self-inflicted yield bug, NOT a security/fabrication issue (read-only public search APIs; the VERIFY gate still governs every record downstream). Surfaced by the Phase-5 final whole-branch review. Fix if `--segment` ever becomes non-interactive/automated input: strip query-operator chars (`:`, parens) + collapse whitespace via a single `segment = segment.strip()` normalization at the CLI/pipeline boundary (also closes the whitespace-only-segment edge in the sonar prompt), and/or tighten the `--segment` help to "plain keywords only." Until then it's Sean's own arg against read-only APIs. — assigned: Sean
- discovery Fusion request-shape compatibility watch (Phase-5 close-out research, LOW): `_build_body` in [tools/llm-council/council/discovery/fusion.py](tools/llm-council/council/discovery/fusion.py) sends the Fusion config as a TOP-LEVEL `"fusion"` block + top-level `model`, but current OpenRouter docs (https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/features/server-tools/fusion) show it nested under `tools[0].parameters` with the judge as `model` inside. Ours is live-verified working (FUSION_SCHEMA.md), and no test would catch a regression if OpenRouter ever drops the top-level form. Fix-if-it-breaks: move config into `tools[0].parameters`. Add a periodic live re-verify (e.g., a quick run after any OpenRouter Fusion changelog). Surfaced by the 2026-06-21 close-out research dig. — assigned: Sean
- ✅ DONE 2026-06-28 (Step-A hygiene, branch `feat/discovery-step-a-hygiene`): `fuse()` now runs `_find_failure_reason` (a defensive scan, since FUSION_SCHEMA.md captured only the success shape) over the last 200 payload and appends any typed `failure_reason` to the FusionError message. ORIGINAL — discovery surface Fusion typed failure_reason (Phase-5 close-out research, LOW diagnosability): the Fusion server tool hard-fails with `status:"error"` + a typed `failure_reason` (all_panels_failed / insufficient_credits / rate_limited / fusion_invocation_capped / unexpected_error). Because `fuse()` reads the outer judge's `message.content` rather than the raw tool result, those reach us as a vague "did not return parseable" + one retry → `FusionError`. Safe (degrades correctly) but loses the real reason. Enhancement: detect `status`/`failure_reason` in the response (if exposed at our layer) and surface it in the FusionError message for diagnosability. Surfaced by the 2026-06-21 close-out research. — assigned: Sean
- discovery last30 HackerNews errors under --no-native-web (Phase-5 close-out, LOW): the 2026-06-21 last30 verification run yielded reddit (3 records) but HN emitted a `hackernews_error` field (0 HN records). Likely the `--no-native-web` flag our collector passes ([tools/llm-council/council/discovery/gather/last30.py](tools/llm-council/council/discovery/gather/last30.py) `_subprocess_runner`) disables a path HN needs. Degrades safely (parser handles the empty hackernews list). Investigate when convenient: try dropping `--no-native-web` for HN, or confirm HN needs native web; weigh against latency/cost. reddit alone already restores meaningful social evidence. **DECISION 2026-06-28 (Step-A): keep `--no-native-web` — it's a deliberate speed/cost choice (paired with `--quick`) applied to all sources; HN needs the native-web path so it degrades safely to empty while reddit (API) yields. Removing it would re-enable native web for everything (slower/heavier) just to recover HN, and it's an untested change against the external last30days plugin. Not worth it — revisit only if HN evidence becomes load-bearing.** — assigned: Sean
- discovery per-run cost cap can be exceeded by actual spend (Phase-5 close-out live finding, MEDIUM): the per-run cap is enforced ONLY on the pre-flight ESTIMATE (`estimated = max_cost_per_run * 0.6`) in [tools/llm-council/council/discovery/__main__.py](tools/llm-council/council/discovery/__main__.py), never against the actual `usage.cost`. A 2026-06-21 standard run on "Claude plugins and skills for 2D animation pipelines" cost **$2.74** vs the $1.50 standard cap (1.8x over); cost is web-tool-call-dominated and high-variance by topic (a deep run the same day was only $0.99). The daily cap ($10) IS checked against actual accumulated spend and remains the real guardrail (close-out testing hit $7.31/$10). Not a correctness/fabrication issue — a budget-predictability one. Options if tighter per-run control is wanted: (a) raise the estimate factor (0.6 → ~1.0) so the pre-flight gate is less optimistic; (b) print a post-run warning when actual > per_run_cap; (c) bound evidence-bundle size / `max_tool_calls` more aggressively per tier; (d) accept it and lean on the daily cap (current design). Surfaced by the close-out confidence runs. — assigned: Sean

- crunchbase-prep / signal-scanner (deferred build — Sean curious, NOT greenlit, 2026-06-23): repurpose the fusion-discovery-council GATHER stage (last30days + Sonar + Exa/Brave + GitHub Issues collectors) + the OpenRouter client + budget spine into a small "leading-signal scanner" = Path A from the Crunchbase prediction-mechanics discussion. Pick a ~50-company watchlist, collect time-stamped leading indicators (hiring velocity, GitHub velocity, news/Reddit mentions), and have the panel emit an evidence-attached SIGNAL-STRENGTH read — NOT a fabricated future-event prediction (the verification gate structurally can't cite a future URL; that's the key insight). It's the honest qualitative version of what Crunchbase's predictions engine does, and would double as a Crunchbase-interview portfolio artifact + possible Substack post. Path B (real ML: LLM extracts features → classical classifier over point-in-time snapshots + accrued outcome labels) needs snapshot-collection-over-time + label accrual, so it can't be bootstrapped from a single scrape (cold-start). Claude offered to spec Path A as a signal-scanner skill; pick back up when ready. — assigned: Sean

- **`writing-voice-modes` emits a pompous comedic construction Sean rejects (surfaced 2026-07-30, VoicePrint refine round 1, decision E3):** the family `which is <someone>'s way of saying <punchline>` and its variants. His words: "I'm also noticing that phrasing happening a lot within the writing voice mode skill. I think it's coming from the David Sedaris calibration or something. That kind of sentence opening keeps happening when trying to add comedy to a description. 'Which is her way of saying I smell like a foot' or 'Which is their way of thinking it was a catastrophe'. It sounds pompous and presumptuous. 'Oh, yes, I know EXACTLY what they're trying to say'. Just constantly adding those to sentences reaching for a silly bit. I don't really like it that much and it doesn't sound like me." The construction claims access to another party's interior then performs the translation, which is the opposite posture from his under-reaction comedy. - **WS3 home page collided with itself from 768px to 1366px — FIXED, and the coarse half had never been looked at** (2026-08-17): Found while chasing the segerman audit's "type-scale inversion" to its cause; the inversion was the **symptom**. On the **home page only**, the headline ran underneath the project tiles, the Projects index ran across them, and the statement and footer overlapped them — violating DESIGN §5's hard rule **L26/1** ("the headline never runs under the media column"), which had been stated since the type scale was authored and **never had a test behind it**. **Measured range**: broken at every width **768–1099 with a fine pointer** and **768–1366 with a coarse one** — every tablet in landscape. Clean at every width ≥1100 fine. Sean's phone is ≤767 and stacks correctly, which is why a real device ran this site end to end without ever showing it. **Scope is home alone, and the reason is structural**: home is the only page laid out with **absolute positioning**, so it cannot reflow, and its single-column escape hatch was scoped to `max-width: 767px`; project and About are CSS grid and were clean at every cell measured. **Root cause in one sentence: the layout and the type scale disagreed about where a band ends.** The root font-size switches at 1099 and at `(pointer: coarse) and (max-width: 1366px)`; the layout switched only at 767, so the inflated root grew the **rem-based** hero column (`--col-hero: 69rem` = 942px at coarse 1366) while the **viewport-percentage** media column (`--x-media: 46%` = 628px) stayed put. **Fix: one media query**, mirroring the root's own bands — the stacked composition already existed, already in reading order, and had simply been unreachable above 767px. `--col-hero: 100%` moved to the same band so the two rules cannot drift apart again. **Coverage: page check 18** (sweeps 360–1920 in 40px steps across all three page types, cross-checking resize against full reloads because a sweep whose two modes disagree measures nothing) **and a new CDP check** for the coarse-pointer band, which no page harness can emulate. **Both were watched failing on the real bug — no fault had to be induced, because the fault was live** — check 18 red on `main` at run `f82stk` (`widths=120 collisions=8`, 800→1080, `resizeVsReload=agree`), the CDP check red at all four tablet widths with `coarse=true` and roots 10.24 / 11.8 / 12.8 / 13.66 — and check 18 flips under `--selftest` (run `fth4tl`, `collisions=19`) by re-applying absolute positioning in the live document rather than faking its own failure. Both were then re-reddened together by hand-reverting the media query (run `fnz0ar`) before the fix was restored. **Gate is now 17 page + 9 CDP, green in Chrome `ferhtr`, Safari 26.5.2 `f5m9rz` and Firefox `fibko7`**; 72 unit tests, `astro check` 0 errors, 11 pages built. **This corrects the audit**: `diff.md` row T2 and DESIGN §5.1 graded the 1080→1120 inversion *deliberate (inherited)* because the reference has identical cliffs at identical widths — true but incomplete, and the obvious remedy (bridging the desktop band up to meet the tablet band) **would have propagated the collision to every desktop width**. **The lesson worth keeping: the audit measured type sizes and box widths and never asked whether two boxes overlap.** Measuring for collision is what found it. **Left deliberately open**: the stacked block's `gap`/`padding` were tuned for a 390px phone and have not been re-judged at 1000–1366 — a green collision check says nothing about whether it *looks* right, so that is Sean's feel gate, not a regression.
- **WS3 display scale DECIDED and shipped — and executing it found the same disease twice more, incl. a live phone defect nobody had seen** (2026-08-17): The audit's largest single ratio, closed. Inner-page display size was `calc(var(--fs-hero) * 0.52)` repeated in `work/[slug].astro` and `info.astro`, absent from DESIGN.md entirely — at 1280 our About title was **3.95× smaller** than the reference's and our project title **2.16×**, while our home matched his to **3%**. Now **`--fs-title-about` 1.15× / `--fs-title-project` 0.85×**, named in `tokens.css` with the reason beside them: measured on the built site at **67.3704px** and **91.1481px**, inside 0.1px of spec. **The order About > home > project diverges from the reference (About > project > home) deliberately** — Sean's call that on a project page the work hits first, so the title reads second. **Both ratios sit below his on purpose**: his display face is weight 400 at +0.74 tracking, ours is Anybody 900 at −0.79. **A test pins the architecture, not just the values** — it fails if any page component derives a display size with its own `calc()`, the exact shape of the original drift. **Renames**: Agent Fleet Observability → **Agent Dash**, Vault Synthesizer Eval Suite → **Vault Evals**, Intent Engineering MCP → **Intent MCP**; longest title 28 chars → 11; slugs untouched and asserted, since they carry URLs, asset filenames, motion-fit keys and `view-transition-name`. **THE SPEC WAS DEFECTIVE AND MEASUREMENT CAUGHT IT.** Its "Verified before speccing" section measured **one width, 1280**, and generalised with a sentence true only *within* a band. Across all three bands the ratios break **every width below 1100**: project **105.5%** of its column at 768–1099, About **149.5%** with the *T* hidden behind the portrait, and on a phone project **140%** plus a sideways-scrolling page. **Neither cause was the ratio.** (1) **768–1099 is the home-collision defect on the other two pages** — both inner pages carry a stacked composition scoped to `max-width: 767px`, the identical structure found on home, while the root switches bands at 1099; `.work`'s head column is crushed to ~63% of the viewport and `.info`'s three rem columns (`52rem + 32rem + auto` + gaps + edges) over-subscribe it by ~112% so `minmax(0, …)` collapses the head to ~28%. Fixed with **the same one-line media query, now on all three pages**; both stacked grids also moved `1fr` → `minmax(0, 1fr)`, because bare `1fr` has a min-content floor and an unbreakable word inflates its own column, hiding its overflow from any element-level check. (2) **≤767 was ALREADY BROKEN before this session, on home, and nobody had ever looked** — "MANAGER" is one unbreakable word **354px wide inside a 308px column at 390px**, running off the right edge of **every phone from 360 to 767** and scrolling the page sideways. **Verified against the pre-session build (`3e2048e`) in a throwaway worktree rather than assumed**, so "pre-existing" is measured, not claimed. Sean saw it in a screenshot of his own phone width. **His call, taken against rendered specimens of the real face at 390px in a picker console: condense rather than shrink** — `--wdth-hero` **95 → 82 on phones only**, so the headline keeps its full 69px and the letters pull in, using an axis the display face already carries and the site had only ever set once; `--fs-title-project` follows at **0.70×** because "Groundwork" is the longest title and sets the ceiling. Options A (smaller type) and C (both, gently) were rendered and rejected. **New coverage: floors check 19** — display text against its own column **plus** a document-level sideways-scroll assertion, because when a box stretches to an unbreakable word the element-level test reads clean (`/work/groundwork/` at 390 reported `clientWidth === scrollWidth === 431` inside a 471px document). Watched failing first (`fynhmg`, `overflow=27 sideways=22`) and flips under `--selftest` (`fzorhh`, `overflow=112 sideways=49`) by doubling the display token in the live document. **Gate is now 18 page + 9 CDP, green in Chrome `f9n65d`, Safari `fwe8xh`, Firefox `fofne4`**; **78 unit tests** (the plan predicted 77; a sixth assertion pins the phone band), `astro check` 0 errors, 11 pages built. **The lesson, and it is the third instance of one disease**: L26/1's box collision, the inner titles below 1100, and the phone headline are all the same disagreement — a **rem** type token driven by the root against a layout column that does **not** move with it. Check 18 measures box-vs-box and is structurally blind to a headline overflowing its own column, because that collides with nothing; check 19 is its sibling. **Non-goal 4 of the spec ("no second step is added") is overturned, by fit rather than taste.** **Left open**: the stacked band's gaps/padding at 768–1366 were tuned for a 390px phone and now apply to project and About as well as home — Sean's feel gate, not a regression.
- **WS3 spec 2 (the three layouts) DESIGNED, SPECCED, PLANNED and handed off — plus the twist that turned out to be the content, not the layout** (2026-08-17): Third of the audit's four decisions. **Not executed** — [spec](../../../seanwinslow.com/docs/plans/2026-08-18-three-layouts-spec.md) `1a4cce0`, [plan](../../../seanwinslow.com/docs/plans/2026-08-18-three-layouts-plan.md) `d5e56c6`+`56f99a2`, [kickoff for a fresh inline session](../../../seanwinslow.com/docs/prompts/2026-08-19-execute-three-layouts-kickoff.md) `3e9f44a`. **What it builds**: home becomes a film strip (tile gap `6rem` → **`2.4rem`**, the reference's own number, captions off); the project page takes his composition (**title full-width → media band → description and metas in one bottom row**, sticky Projects index removed, `← Projects` affordance replacing it); About becomes **four quadrants** (title top-left, portrait right, prose bottom-left, metas bottom-right) with metas **Tools / Record / Elsewhere / Available** — where the reference lists Awards we list receipts, each with its measurement date, and Available moves up out of the fixed footer. Markup and CSS only: no JS, no dependency, **no new media by design**. Gate 18+9 → **19+9**. **THE SESSION'S REAL FINDING IS THE TWIST.** Sean picked the "drop the Projects list" option and then reframed the whole thing: *"the twist is the actual content we're presenting and how we present it… segerman's showing screen recordings of the websites he built… We should give each project their own personality and do the same thing, but SHOW the projects through the visuals… One of the biggest things we have to get across is my judgement and reasoning behind my decision making."* **His premise was researched before it was designed against, and it survived two independent sources** ([research doc](../../../seanwinslow.com/docs/plans/2026-08-18-judgment-layer-research.md), `a46aab8`, everything in it marked READ rather than measured). Nate Jones' Executive Circle archive opens on a reader with Sean's exact problem — *"the work they were proudest of was the work they could not show"* — and lands on *"the work sample is no longer enough; **the thinking layer has to travel with the work**"*. Public hiring commentary states Sean's premise almost verbatim from the employer side: *"building is dramatically cheaper with AI, which shifts the bottleneck from execution to judgment — when shipping is no longer the hard part, the question becomes whether you knew what to ship and why."* **The usable output is Nate's four questions, adopted as a house grammar for the media band**: situation → **the fork (what was rejected)** → the catch (the risk, because *"a lot of good judgment looks like nothing happened"*) → what changed, with a real number and its measurement date. Beat 2 is the one no portfolio shows and is exactly what a finished artifact hides; beat 3 is *why* these projects resist a screen recording at all. **The content largely already exists in code-brain's own CLAUDE.md** — $1.16/file vs $0, eight of ten agents disabled after an audit found two producing value, `fallback = "none"` so an off-hours miss never silently bills the paid API. **Sean's assumption that we were already looping the film strip was WRONG and the correction matters**: the loop is DESIGN §5.1 **decision 4**, recorded as open, never built — his home is 800px and does not scroll at all (wheel never moves `scrollY`; at 4,800px of wheel the first image is back), ours is a 2,191px document with 1,391px of scroll. **The tight gap is downstream of the loop, not a substitute for it** — which is why 2.4rem felt obviously right to him and read as risky to me. **Carried into spec 3 as the sharp hazard: a loop that clones tiles clones their `view-transition-name`, and a duplicate silently kills the morph** — precisely what floors check 16 exists to catch. **Dropping the captions superseded a recorded reason** (§5 named the plate rather than the `<figure>` *because the figure carried a caption on home and none on a work page*); it now has two better ones, Sean's leading — a strip that loops cannot stutter the same seven captions past you every 4,800px, and `view-transition-name` permanently makes an element a stacking context and backdrop root, which on the plate is confined to the picture and on the `<figure>` would constrain three separate compositions. **Two defects were found in my own plan during self-review against the real source and fixed before handoff** (`56f99a2`): check 20's index selector double-counted the component and its page-side wrapper, so `/work/` would have reported 2 against a plan predicting 1 with no way to tell a wrong selector from a failed removal; and a step said to add rules "beside the existing `.chrome__name` rules" when there are none. **Left open for Sean at the feel gate**: the strip at 2.4rem is specced for a reel spec 3 has not built yet (contact-sheet is the plausible failure, one-number fix); whether `Contact` becomes a fifth meta if the character signature reads orphaned in the footer; and the stacked tile gap at 768–1366, deliberately left at `5rem` to be judged on a device rather than derived.
- **WS3 spec 3 (the looping film strip + the warp) SPECCED — the last of the audit's four decisions, and it ships with its own exit ramp** (2026-08-17): [spec](../../../seanwinslow.com/docs/plans/2026-08-19-loop-and-warp-spec.md) `73569af`. **Needs a plan, not a brainstorm — the decisions are taken.** DESIGN §5.1 **decision 4**, the one the audit explicitly flagged *"do not decide it in a hurry"*, plus **decision 3** (route transition 520ms → **1200ms**) folded in because it is the same moment and would otherwise mean building the velocity signal twice. **Depends on spec 2 having shipped**: it needs the `2.4rem` gap and the removed captions, because a caption repeating every cycle is the tell that turns a reel back into a list. **THE SCROLL MODEL WAS THE WHOLE DECISION, and Sean chose B.** Three options were put to him: (A) his one-screen fixed shell — `html` fixed, wheel captured, genuinely infinite, and by far the most invasive change this site could make; (B) **a `position: sticky` pinned section inside a document that still scrolls natively**; (C) a recycling column with no pinning. **B was chosen so the accessibility floors keep working without rescue** — keyboard paging, screen-reader virtual cursors, find-in-page and `prefers-reduced-motion` all work today precisely because nothing is hijacked. **A is staged rather than rejected**: the accumulator, reel and wrap are the same machinery A needs, which is what makes the retreat a shell swap instead of a rewrite. **The velocity signal is ported, not invented** — Session A recovered the whole chain from his shipped bundles: wheel clamped to ±100, ×0.6 into an unbounded accumulator, lerp 0.07/frame, ×7.5, a second delta-corrected filter at base 0.3, and **absolute speed under 0.05 snapping to zero** (that last one is what stops the warp shimmering at rest and must be ported with the rest). **Lenis is NOT adopted** — on his site it is only the desktop input normalizer and he owns the accumulator himself; DESIGN §5's Lenis bullet is a licence, not an obligation. **THE WARP IS A LADDER, because his warp is a bend and a CSS transform cannot bend**: his shader scales each vertex's x-position by squared distance from centre × `0.2 × uSpeed`, so edges travel further than the middle and it reverses with direction, and DESIGN §5 already records the ceiling (*"a linear CSS skew past ~1.5° reads as a cheap italic rather than a bend"*) — written before anyone had his numbers. Rungs: **1** `perspective` + `rotateY`, which is *projective* and therefore non-linear in the same direction his is, and which DESIGN never considered because it predates the measurements; **2** SVG `feDisplacementMap`, a true per-pixel bend; **3** canvas in vertical slices, literally a software vertex shader; **4** WebGL. **Rung 4 is named as a CLIFF, not a step**: his WebGL meshes are precisely why he needs barba, so moving our tiles into WebGL re-opens the route-transition architecture DESIGN §5 settled deliberately, the zero-JS work-page payload, and the four floors checks built around them. It is a decision to be taken at the top of a session with the cost stated, never the next thing tried when rung 3 disappoints. **Also recorded: his home and project pages warp on DIFFERENT axes** — home bends the sides (`5e-5`, squared distance from centre, applied to x), project bends top and bottom (`2e-5` → ×200 → ×0.6 = effective `0.0024`, squared vertical position). Two effects, not one. **SEAN'S OWN REQUIREMENT SHAPED THE SPEC**: *"if I play with it and find the ending or the warp feels off, we should take a step back and figure out how to properly set up the project so we don't keep running into issues because we went down a different path."* So the spec names **five failure conditions in measurable terms before any code**, each with a pre-agreed move: F1 warp reads as italic → climb the ladder, keep the loop; F2 p95 frame time crosses **16.7ms** (real baseline to fail against — this site measures p95 9.9/10.2/10.2ms through the handoff and a locked p50 17/p95 17 over 645 frames on his iPhone) → drop a rung or restrict the warp to the hovered tile; F3 visible seam → a bug, not a pivot; F4 the ending reads badly → **escalate B to A**; F5 any accessibility floor breaks → stop and fix, no floor is traded for an effect. **Budget: +12 KB gzip on home, hard** (home ships 9,762 B today); work pages stay byte-unchanged at 7,680 B with zero external JS, because the reel is home-only. **Three things flagged as NOT verified rather than assumed**, and they are the plan's first instrumentation: `position: sticky` inside the intro's transformed `<main>` (the armed state parks it at `translateY(100vh)`, and a transformed ancestor changes how sticky resolves); `view-transition-name` on an element inside a sticky, translated subtree; and whether rung 1 is enough, which nobody has seen. **New coverage the plan must add**: check 16 green **mid-wrap** (the clone hazard), check 15 clicking a tile **after the reel has travelled**, no warp under reduced motion or keyboard scrolling, and frame budget under scroll at 1× and 4× throttle.
- **WS3 floors check 15 is INTERMITTENTLY RED and nobody knows why — the harness's own credibility is the thing at risk** (2026-08-17): Found while reviewing Codex's spec 2 branch: `npm run floors` reported `check=15 verdict=BAD` (`run=fow8db`) claiming the tile→hero morph did not happen — *"no `::view-transition-group(scene-…)` animated, so the root crossfaded and the tile did NOT morph"*, with all seven `::view-transition-old(scene-*)` present and **no `new` or `group`** for the clicked pair. **It is NOT a regression and NOT caused by spec 2.** Established by four independent measurements: the `view-transition-name` is present and unique on both sides (`grep` of `dist`), floors **check 16 was green in the same run** (`/ names=7 | /work/code-brain/ names=1`), a **live-transition probe hooking `pagereveal` on the destination** shows `::view-transition-group(scene-code-brain)` **and** `::view-transition-new(scene-code-brain)` both present at **1280×800, 820×540, 820×900 and 1280×540** (`MORPHED=yes` at all four), and the branch then ran **green twice in a row** (`fskwee`, `f01wbh`). **`main` flakes at the same rate — 1 red in 3 runs** — so it predates spec 2 entirely. **Why this matters more than a flaky test usually does**: this repo's standing lesson is *"a green check is a claim"*, and check 15 exists precisely because **four distinct faults all look like an ordinary successful navigation** and none of them throws or logs. An intermittently-red check inverts that protection — the next genuine breakage gets waved through as "just the flake", which is the exact failure mode the harness was built to prevent. **Also observed the same day and also unexplained**: one `npm run floors -- --firefox` run reported `cdp=RED` while its page suite was green, not reproduced in three subsequent runs, failing check name not captured. **Two intermittents in one day in a harness whose whole value is trustworthiness.** **Suspected mechanism, untested**: check 15 clicks a tile *while its hover clip is playing* (`clipPlaying=true` in every run) and reads `document.getAnimations()` at `ready`; a race between the still↔clip crossfade (`--dur-mode`) and the snapshot would produce exactly this signature — the outgoing document captures fine, the destination contributes nothing. Spec 2 plausibly *widens* the window without causing it, since the work page's media band went full-width and the destination snapshot is now 663px tall against 307px before. **Job**: reproduce deliberately (loop `npm run floors -- --no-cdp` 10× and count), then either fix the race or make check 15 retry-once-and-report rather than fail — but **NOT** silently, since a check that retries until green is worse than one that flakes. Until then, spec 3's plan tells its implementer to re-run once before investigating a check-15 red, which is a workaround and is recorded as one.

- **WS3 route transitions SHIPPED — the tile becomes the project, at zero JS; research was the gate and it moved a line in §5** (2026-08-16): DESIGN §5's second build-order item, and the thing that delivers the project-page reveals Sean flagged on segerman.dev. Pure-CSS cross-document `@view-transition`, no JavaScript, no dependency, gated behind `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`. **Research ran as a gate, not a step, and earned it.** §5's line "Firefox gets today's instant navigation" is still TRUE — but "Firefox does not support view transitions" is now FALSE: Firefox **144 shipped the same-document half** (`view-transition-name`, `startViewTransition`, the pseudo-elements, `view-transition-class`, `:active-view-transition`) and none of the cross-document half; Mozilla's meta bug 1860854 was `NEW` with no milestone as of 2026-08-04. Stopping at "Firefox 144" in the compat table would have marked a true sentence stale and re-opened a settled architecture. Every feature used has the same floor, **Safari 18.2**, so there is no tier between all of it and none of it. **Three decisions, each made from a measurement rather than an adjective.** (1) *The name goes on the PLATE, not the `<figure>`* — the figure carries a `<figcaption>` on home and none on a work page, so naming it would name two boxes of different aspect ratios, and these snapshots are bitmaps stretched to their group with `object-fit: fill` by default. (2) *The six non-morphing tiles are the decision, and the default is not "nothing"* — naming an element pulls it out of the root snapshot into its own top-layer group, so left alone six tiles hang over the destination page for 520ms, **350ms after the page they belong to has gone**. They now leave with the page, discriminated in pure CSS by `:only-child` (an unpaired capture is the only child of its image pair), proved by giving those six a 999ms duration and reading it back off `getAnimations()`. (3) *The page does not crossfade* — it leaves, bare paper shows for 150ms, the next page arrives; a plain crossfade puts two 107px display headlines on screen together and the double exposure is visible in stepped frames. **No new easing curve**: the plate travels on `--ease-sweep` (minted in §5 for exactly this shape of move) and the two pictures hand over on `--dur-mode`, which tokens.css already defines as "the tile still-to-motion crossfade" — a tile clicked with a fine pointer *is* showing its clip. `--ease-pop` untouched. **Every navigation path has a decided answer**: home tile → hero morphs; a Projects-column entry morphs the corresponding tile, including one 2030px below the fold (measured: decelerates into frame at ~40% of the duration); work → work is an in-place dissolve because both plates are `:only-child` in the *same box*, so the plate holds as a frame while the words change — better than a morph and needing no rule; `/info/` and 404 opt in for the root fade; a deep link has no origin document and correctly gets nothing. **Coverage 13→16 page checks and 7→8 CDP checks, all green in Chrome 151 AND real Safari 26.5.2**, plus 72 unit tests and a clean `astro check`. Four new checks rather than one because **four separate faults produce an identical, entirely normal-looking navigation** — no opt-in on either page, a duplicate `view-transition-name`, no engine support, reduced motion — and none of them throws or logs, and cross-document leaves no promise to reject. Check 15 asserts the only two things a live transition emits (a `ViewTransition` on `pagereveal`, a `::view-transition-group(scene-…)` alive at `ready`) and clicks the tile while its clip plays; check 16 reads computed names off every element of five documents; check 17 is the §9 tile-click contract nothing had ever tested; CDP 8 runs the same click with reduced motion on and off and demands both answers, because an off-only assertion passes against a completely broken build. **The harness needed a seam, not a driver** (same shape as `?nowebgl2`): `pagereveal` fires in the incoming document before first render and the parent cannot hook it, because a same-origin navigation keeps the **same WindowProxy** — the first version of check 15 reported `pagereveal=false` against a site where the transition was demonstrably running. `scripts/vt-probe.js` is served from outside `dist` and injected by the harness server on a **cookie**, since a real link click navigates to a bare URL and only the destination can answer. Settled first by experiment: cross-document transitions **do** run inside a same-origin iframe, which is what keeps these checks where real Safari can run them. **Check 17 found a fact about the INTRO nobody had named**: the armed state parks `<main>` at `translateY(100vh)`, so the tiles are not at any viewport coordinate at all — `elementFromPoint` returned null, which was the belt reporting itself. Three belts now measured, with which one holds when: geometry and the cover while loading, `inert` **alone** at `settled` where the cover takes `pointer-events:none`. **All four watched failing first**: `--selftest` flips all three page checks (16 induces a *real* duplicate rather than faking the result); check 15 also run against a build with the opt-in deleted (`viewTransition=false`, `sceneGroups=[]`); CDP 8 run against an inverted gate and failed in **both** directions. **Also measured and deliberately left alone**: the paper grain drops out for the length of a transition — 0.0517 vs 0.4889 high-frequency energy over bare paper, because the pseudo tree renders in the top layer above the fixed grain. Under half a grey level; recorded so it is not rediscovered. Commits `6c1a105` `206a5c0` `d12dc00`. **Open for Sean, neither blocking.** (a) *The feel gate* — production build served on the LAN at `http://192.168.68.54:8123/` with a stepped-frame console at the artifact link in that session; three calls are flippable in a minute (fade-through-paper vs crossfade, whether the other six tiles get their own exit, the 520ms duration). **Sean's two calls, taken this session and recorded in §5:** the work page gets **no arrival beat** with this (the morph ships alone; the headline write-on stays where the build order puts it, next — and the repo has no footage of the reference's project pages to judge sufficiency against, only the already-mined intro clip), and **Firefox stays unverified for now** (it was not installed on this Mac, so closing the gap meant installing a browser and that was not mine to do). **Sean then installed Firefox 153.0.4 the same evening**, and a reconnaissance pass of the page suite was run in it before the next session's prompt was written: **15 of 16 green, check 15 the only red, and it fails correctly** — Firefox has no cross-document view transitions so `pagereveal=false`, while the same run shows the navigation landing on `/work/code-brain/` with the right `<h1>`, which is §5's Firefox contract observed rather than assumed for the first time. The ticket is **not** closed: `npm run floors` has no `--firefox`, the CDP suite cannot reach Gecko, and one manual pass is not a gate. **Still unverified, unchanged in shape and larger by one**: Firefox has still never been run; touch is still untested (and a tap matters slightly more now, since a fall-through would be a navigation — three belts stop it, all tested, but in a desktop browser rather than under a thumb); reduced motion in WebKit is still unverified, which now also means the reduced-motion half of route transitions is checked in Chrome and **not** in Safari, since no page harness can set that media feature. **AMENDED 2026-08-16 (later): re-running the gate to verify these numbers rather than inherit them found a defect in the gate itself — commit `a12ab99`.** `npm run floors` reported *"the page suite never reported"* while the suite was, in the same moment, **green**. Third time in this harness the *instrument* rather than the site was at fault, and the first time in the **runner** rather than in a check. Mechanism: the standalone server that hands Sean's phone a URL is deliberately left on port 8123, so a second server there is normal workflow — `floors.mjs` spawned its own with `stdio:'ignore'` so the `EADDRINUSE` was invisible, its readiness probe was a `GET /` that the **foreign** server answered 200, and `intro-check-server.mjs` truncated its log *before* `.listen()`, leaving a one-line log that read exactly like a run which produced nothing. Every beacon went to the other server's log. **The quiet direction was the dangerous one:** `ROOT` is `cwd/dist` of whichever process won the bind, so a server left up in another checkout serves a *different site* and the run would have scored it as the one just built — §5's standing lesson arriving through the runner instead of a check. Fix: the server answers `/__whoami` with `{pid, root, log, port}`, truncates only after a successful bind, and fails loudly on stderr; the runner **adopts** a stranger provably serving the same `dist` (killing it would take down the URL Sean's phone is on) and **refuses** one that is not, or one that will not identify itself, naming both roots and the command that clears it. All three branches watched, not reasoned about: pre-handshake server → refused with no stray log; same dist → adopted pid 92618, `run=f5wv3d ALL checks=16 bad=0 verdict=GREEN`; different dist → refused printing both roots. **The generalisation is not about servers:** a readiness probe any listener can satisfy asks whether *something* is there, when the gate needs to know whether *the right thing* is there. **Re-verified after the fix, both engines: Chrome 151 `run=f5wv3d` 16/0 GREEN, Safari 26.5.2 `run=fbd8sq` 16/0 GREEN, CDP 8/0 GREEN, `FLOORS GREEN`, 72 unit tests, `astro check` 0 errors.** **Still the one open item: Sean's feel gate** — the production build is live at `http://localhost:8123/` and `http://192.168.68.54:8123/` (phone, same LAN), and the three flippable calls are unchanged: fade-through-paper vs plain crossfade, whether the six non-morphing tiles get a beat of their own, and the 520ms travel. **FEEL GATE CLOSED 2026-08-16 — ship as built.** Sean judged it on the production build (not a dev server); all three flippable calls put to him with their costs stated rather than their merits — the page hand-off (fade-through-paper vs plain crossfade, cost = the measured ~100ms double exposure of two 107px headlines), whether the six non-morphing tiles get a beat of their own, and the 520ms travel. **His call on all three: unchanged.** The choreography is now judged as well as measured, so nothing here is open. Moved to Done; the remaining gaps (Firefox in the gate, touch, WebKit reduced motion) are carried by the text-arrival-beats ticket and DESIGN §5, not by this one.

- **anima front door: capture Sean's one-line reason at each lock (surfaced 2026-07-30, creative-harness T27a — provenance only, details stay in-lane):** Sean narrowly lifted the "Anima is read-only" rule for exactly this edit. Job: update `anima/.claude/skills/brainstorm-front-door/SKILL.md` (Step 0 lock discipline) and `references/session-sidecar-contract.md` so the orchestrator asks *why* in one line whenever it writes a LOCKED DECISION and records the answer in the lock entry — reason capture at lock time, nothing else; write-back stays out of scope. His reason for wanting the capture everywhere: "It's meant to help me think differently and creatively and make me better for various topics and tasks." **Change contract added 2026-07-31 (external adversarial review, Sean-ratified):** the allowed surface is exactly these two files — no art-department edits, no shared-dependency edits, diff kept to that allowlist; acceptance test = the only new persisted datum is a user-authored reason on a front-door lock. Anything broader is a new lift Sean must approve first. **Verbatim-marking requirement added 2026-08-02 (creative-harness T28 grilling, Sean-ratified — provenance only):** the captured reason must be recorded as Sean's own words and explicitly marked verbatim in the lock entry (the sidecar's existing `(verbatim)` convention, as SPARK lines already use) — an orchestrator paraphrase does not satisfy the requirement, and downstream consumers will treat unmarked reason text as paraphrase, which defeats the point of capturing at lock time. **Gate status: this edit is now unblocked** — the design gate it waited on ratified 2026-08-02. **DONE 2026-08-02** — applied to both allowlisted files committed as `290e1ee` and shipped to anima `main` via [PR #121](https://github.com/seanwinslow28/anima/pull/121) (squash-merged 2026-08-02 as `454b766`; both rule blocks verified present on `main`). Five design choices grilled and ratified in-session: (1) fixed ask wording "Why that one? One line."; (2) the reason lands as an indented `- why (verbatim): "…"` sub-line under the lock, reusing the sidecar's existing `(verbatim)` marker rather than inventing a second idiom; (3) on a category-grade answer the orchestrator offers **one** candidate reason as a thinking prompt (Sean's pick over open pushback — "it adds to the brainstorming aspect. I could like an option, but not really find the reason why. Having the orchestrator make a guess could help get the brain churning."); (4) a bare agreement with that guess records **nothing** — the guess is never promoted into the quote, which is what keeps the acceptance test intact; (5) `SUPERSEDES` entries take the sub-line too, alongside the entry line's existing prose why (orchestrator's account of what changed vs Sean's reason for changing it). Skip and silence record nothing in every path. Verified: `git -C anima diff --stat` touches only the two allowlisted files, no new emitted field or schema surface, no private-lane vocabulary in the diff. — assigned: Sean

- DONE 2026-06-18 (Claude Code): "test suite hang" on the Gemini DR call resolved — `pytest tests/` now completes (895 passed in ~13s, was hanging). Root cause was subtler than reported: `test_run_warn_called_with_mtd_plus_pred` in [agents-sdk/tests/test_gemini_dr.py](agents-sdk/tests/test_gemini_dr.py) pre-seeded the ledger with a hard-coded **2026-05-03** entry, but `run()` computes mtd/today against the live `date.today()`. Once real time passed into June the May entry stopped counting, the daily-cap refusal no longer fired, and execution fell through to the **real** `client.interactions.create()` (genai + get_credential were unpatched) — a live DR call that polls up to 3900s. Fix: dated the ledger entry to `date.today()` so the cap math engages (also restores the intended $12.80 warn assertion), and added hermetic `patch("scripts.gemini_dr.genai")` + `get_credential` guards plus `mock_genai.Client.assert_not_called()` so no test can ever reach the network again. NOTE: code lives outside the vault and is uncommitted in the working tree — commit when ready.
